<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Generalised Abundance Conjecture: if $`(X,B)`$ is a projective klt ℚ-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, $`K_X+B`$ is pseudo-effective, <i>M</i> is a nef ℚ-Cartier divisor on <i>X</i>, and $`K_X+B+M`$ is nef, then $`K_X+B+M`$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample ℚ-Cartier divisor on <i>X</i>.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If $$D=K_X+B+M$$ is nef, then there is a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $X$ such that $D\equiv L$.
  - Explanation: The nef adjoint has a semiample numerical representative under the stated projective klt, rationality, pseudo-effectivity, and characteristic assumptions.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cy)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair in characteristic zero with $K_X+B\equiv0$. Every nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$ is numerically semiample.
  - Explanation: Numerically trivial klt adjoints specialize the main conclusion to every nef rational Cartier divisor.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-pseudo-vg)): Let $D$ be a pseudo-effective rational Cartier divisor on a projective complex variety $X$. Every integral curve through a sufficiently very general point has nonnegative $D$-degree.
  - Explanation: Effective approximations give nonnegative degrees on curves through sufficiently very general points.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-input-abundance)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective log canonical $\mathbb Q$-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. If $K_X+B$ is nef, then it is semiample.
  - Explanation: Ordinary log abundance is an input concerning an adjoint without a generalized nef summand.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-input-models)): Let $(X,B+\mathbf M)$ be a projective generalized klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over $\mathbb C$, with $X$ $\mathbb Q$-factorial and with fixed nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. If $K_X+B+M_X$ is pseudo-effective, there is a terminating choice of its minimal-model program, with $\mathbb Q$-factorial generalized klt models and a nef endpoint. The program extracts no divisors and generalized log discrepancies do not decrease. On a common resolution of its initial and final models, the pullback of the initial adjoint is the pullback of the final adjoint plus an effective divisor exceptional over the final model.
  - Explanation: The fixed nef b-divisor data admit a terminating choice of program with a nef endpoint and effective exceptional comparison.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound)): Let $(V,\Delta+\mathbf N)$ be a projective generalized klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, with effective boundary, $\mathbb Q$-factorial $V$, and nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. Every $(K_V+\Delta+N_V)$-negative extremal ray has an integral rational curve generator $C$ for which $$-4n\leq (K_V+\Delta+N_V)\cdot C<0.$$ The contraction, and the flip when it is small, can be realized using an ordinary klt adjoint negative on the same ray. Birational MMP outputs remain $\mathbb Q$-factorial. On a common resolution of a birational step, the pullback of the input generalized adjoint equals the pullback of its output transform plus an effective divisor exceptional over the output.
  - Explanation: Generalized negative rays admit bounded rational curve generators and ordinary klt realizations of their birational steps.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-two-nef-model)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, let $T=K_X+B$ be pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. Suppose <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds. Then there are a birational contraction $X\dashrightarrow Y$, a number $s_0\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$, and a common smooth projective resolution $$X\xleftarrow{p}W\xrightarrow{q}Y$$ with the following properties. If $B_Y$ and $N$ denote the transforms of $B$ and $M$, and $U=K_Y+B_Y$, then* 1. *$(Y,B_Y)$ is klt and $\mathbb Q$-factorial, $U$ is semiample, and $\kappa(Y,U)=\kappa(X,T)\geq0$;* 2. *$N$ is pseudo-effective, and $U+s_0N$ is nef of full nef dimension;* 3. *there are effective $q$-exceptional rational divisors $E_0,E_1$ on $W$ such that $$\label{eq:two-comparisons} p^*T=q^*U+E_0, \qquad p^*(T+s_0M)=q^*(U+s_0N)+E_1.$$
  - Explanation: Compatible programs retain an ordinary semiample adjoint and a nearby generalized nef adjoint of full nef dimension.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-full-positive)): Assume $(P_j)$ for $j<n$. Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, let $T=K_X+B$ be pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds and $\kappa(X,T)>0$, then $T+cM$ is big for every $c\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$.
  - Explanation: Positive Iitaka dimension permits induction on fibres to establish bigness of every positive nef perturbation.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-reduction-cy)): Assume $(P_j)$ for $j<n$. Suppose that on every projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair $(V,\Delta)$ of dimension $n$ with $K_V+\Delta\equiv0$, every nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor of full nef dimension is big. Then $(P_n)$ holds.
  - Explanation: The full-dimension induction reduces to bigness on klt pairs with numerically trivial adjoint.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-bounded-curves)): For every integer $f\geq1$ there is an integer $m_f>0$ with the following property. Let $Y$ be a smooth integral projective variety of dimension $f$, let $D_0$ be a reduced simple normal crossing divisor or the zero divisor, and let $L_0$ be a nef integral Cartier divisor of full nef dimension on $Y$. Suppose that $P_0$ is a nef rational Cartier divisor and that $r,C_0,C_1$ are positive real numbers satisfying $$0<P_0^f\leq C_0,\qquad P_0-rL_0\text{ is nef}.$$ If $A_0=K_Y+D_0+L_0$ is big and $$\label{j:adjoint-ratio} A_0\cdot P_0^{f-1}\leq C_1P_0^f,$$ then $$\label{j:bounded-curve-obstruction} r\leq(m_fC_1)^{f-1}C_0.$$ The integer $m_f$ depends only on $f$, and not on the variety or the divisors. For dimensions $1\leq f\leq n$, one may replace all $m_f$ by a single integer $m$ depending only on $n$.
  - Explanation: Polarized birationality and integral positive curve degrees bound the rescaling parameter under the stated intersection hypotheses.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-very-general-images)): Let $X$ be a normal integral projective variety that is not uniruled, and let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor of full nef dimension on $X$. There is a complement $X^\circ$ of a countable union of proper closed subsets of $X$ with the following property. If $Y$ is a smooth integral projective variety and $g:Y\to X$ is generically finite onto a positive-dimensional image meeting $X^\circ$, then $g^*L$ has full nef dimension, $Y$ is not uniruled, and $K_Y$ is pseudo-effective.
  - Explanation: Images meeting the selected very general locus preserve full nef dimension and exclude uniruledness on generically finite smooth sources.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-subadjunction)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety, let $\Theta\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\subset Z$ be integral of dimension $f>0$. Suppose that $(Z,\Theta)$ is log canonical at the generic point of $V$ and has a divisor of log discrepancy zero with center $V$. If $P$ is a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$ and $\rho:V^*\to V$ is a smooth projective resolution factoring through its normalization, then $$K_{V^*}\cdot(\rho^*P)^{f-1} \leq (K_Z+\Theta)\|_V\cdot P^{f-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Generic log canonicity and a log canonical place give a numerical canonical-intersection bound on a smooth resolution of the center.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-base-component)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $R$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer such that $kR$ is Cartier. Let $0\ne\mathcal H\subset H^0(Z,\mathcal O_Z(kR))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\eta_V$, and that $\eta_V\in Z_{\mathrm{sm}}$. Let $\mathfrak m$ be the maximal ideal of $\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}$. If $\mathfrak b\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}\subset\mathfrak m^h$ for an integer $h>0$, there are a rational number $0<c\leq(d-f)k/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\Theta\sim_{\mathbb Q}cR$ such that $(Z,\Theta)$ is log canonical at $\eta_V$ and has a log canonical place centered on $V$. The degree and canonical bounds are $$\label{j:base-bounds} \begin{split} R^f\cdot V&\leq(k/h)^{d-f}R^d,\\ K_{V^*}\cdot R^{f-1} &\leq(K_Z+cR)\|_V\cdot R^{f-1}\qquad(f>0), \end{split}$$ where $V^*$ is a smooth projective resolution. The degree bound also holds for $f=0$.
  - Explanation: An isolated smooth generic base component with high multiplicity yields an effective boundary and degree bounds.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-moving-kernel)): Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, let $R$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $k>0$ such that $kR$ is Cartier. Choose positive real numbers $\tau_0<\cdots<\tau_d$ with consecutive gap $\delta>0$. For $x\in Z_{\mathrm{sm}}$, put $$\mathcal H_i(x)=H^0\bigl(Z,\mathcal O_Z(kR) \otimes\mathfrak m_x^{\lceil k\tau_i\rceil}\bigr).$$ Suppose that for very general $x$ all these spaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\delta\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an integral parameter variety $T$ with a dominant marked-point map $x:T\to Z_{\mathrm{sm}}$, a fixed index $i\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}$, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ containing $x(t)$ and common to the base loci of $\mathcal H_i(x(t))$ and $\mathcal H_{i+1}(x(t))$. In particular, the incidence $$\Gamma=\{(t,z):z\in V_t\}\subset T\times Z$$ dominates $Z$.* *For general $t$, the dimension $f$ of $V_t$ lies in $[1,d-1]$, $V_t$ is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and the entire higher-kernel base ideal is contained there in the ordinary power $\mathcal I_{V_t}^h$, where $$h=\lceil k\delta/2\rceil,\qquad k/h\leq2/\delta.$$ The incidence base ideal is likewise contained in $\mathcal I_\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\Gamma$. These containments are taken in the smooth ambient open. If $Z$ is also $\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt, then $$\label{j:moving-bounds} \begin{split} R^f\cdot V_t&\leq(2/\delta)^{d-f}R^d,\\ K_{V_t^*}\cdot R^{f-1} &\leq(K_Z+cR)\|_{V_t}\cdot R^{f-1}, \qquad 0<c\leq2(d-f)/\delta. \end{split}$$ The top intersection on $V_t$ is positive when $V_t$ meets the open on which the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: Full moving jet kernels yield a dominant incidence family and ordinary-power containment of its higher base ideal.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-fiber-restriction)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#j:moving-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="j:moving-kernel">14</a>, let $g:Z\to B$ be a morphism. There is a dense open of the incidence with the following property. Choose $(t,z)$ in this open, put $b=g(z)$, and suppose that $V_t$ is smooth over a smooth open of its actual image at $z$. Let $F$ be the reduced component through $z$ of the scheme fiber of $V_t\to g(V_t)$. Near $z$ in $Z_b$, the restricted higher-kernel base ideal has support exactly $F$ and is contained in the ordinary power $\mathcal I_F^h$.* *If $F$ is proper in an integral ambient fiber component containing it, the restricted series is nonzero there and $F$ is an isolated base component at its generic point. Applying Lemma <a href="#j:base-component" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="j:base-component">13</a> on that ambient component additionally requires its projectivity, $\mathbb Q$-factoriality, klt singularities, and smoothness at the generic point of $F$.
  - Explanation: At general incidence points, smoothness over the actual image preserves reduced fibre ideals and their ordinary powers.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-j-finite-covers)): Let $Y$ be a normal integral projective variety, let $\mathcal V\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\mathcal V\to Y$ be a morphism. Suppose that the general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$.* *After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\mathrm{sm}}$ to divisors $\mathcal R_1,\ldots,\mathcal R_N$ in $\mathcal V$. Retain this notation after the base change. Let $I$ consist of the indices $j$ for which $\overline{g_t((\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume that $$\overline{g(\mathcal R_j)}\ne Y\qquad(j\in I).$$ After shrinking again, there is a smooth nonempty open $Y^\circ\subset Y$ over which every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ is finite étale. Only finitely many such normal covers of $Y$ occur, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections; the open and the finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.
  - Explanation: Non-sweeping relative branch images supply a fixed étale open and finiteness of bounded-degree normal covers.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-terminal-big)): Let $n\ge2$, and assume $(P_j)$ for $j<n$. Let $X$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal complex variety of dimension $n$ with $K_X\sim0$. If $L$ is a nef Cartier divisor on $X$ with full nef dimension, then $L$ is big.
  - Explanation: The terminal trivial-canonical case establishes bigness from full nef dimension, conditional on the lower-dimensional induction.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-scalar-bound)): In the setting of <a href="#eq:scaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaling">[eq:scaling]</a>, for all sufficiently small $t$ and at a very general point $x\in X$, every nonzero section of $kP$, where $k$ is a positive integer and $kP$ is Cartier, satisfies $$\label{j:scalar-order} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)\le4k\rho.$$
  - Explanation: The hypothetical counterexample scaling bounds vanishing orders of every nonzero section at a very general point.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-large-seshadri)): For a suitable fixed positive integer $q$, and all sufficiently small $t$ in <a href="#eq:scaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaling">[eq:scaling]</a>, there is a smooth point $z\in Z$ in the torus open, lying over smooth points of both factors, such that $$\label{j:large-seshadri} \varepsilon(R;z)>2n+2.$$ More generally, for any prescribed dense open subsets $U_1,U_2$ of the smooth locus of $X$, the point may be chosen over $U_1\times U_2$. In particular, these opens may be the isomorphism loci of fixed resolutions of $X$.
  - Explanation: The projective bundle admits a point with large Seshadri constant, including prescribed smooth open conditions.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-data)): Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\ge2$. Let $D,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\label{fr:intersection-bounds} \begin{gathered} H^n\le(2\epsilon)^n,\qquad K_WH^{n-1}\le2H^n/r,\qquad (2D+qS)H^{n-1}\le4H^n,\\ (14\epsilon)^n<\tfrac14,\qquad 80\epsilon<\tfrac34. \end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.* 1. *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$ for which $l_iH$ is Cartier, with $\mu_{\min}(\Omega_W^1\|_C)\ge0$.* 2. *On the projective bundle $$Z=\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal O_W(S)_1\oplus \mathcal O_W(S)_2\bigr)\longrightarrow W\times W, \qquad \xi=c_1(\mathcal O_Z(1)),\qquad M=D_1+D_2+q\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\mathcal O_Z(a\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum for $a\ge0$; subscripts indicate pullback from the corresponding factor of $W\times W$. The two axes are the sections defined by the two summands. A smooth point $z_*$ in their complement and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0D$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.* 3. *A point $x\in W$, its blowup $\pi_x:\widehat W\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\gamma=f_*(A_1\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\to\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\cdot\gamma>0$ and $$\label{fr:movable-input} \pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\lambda S)\cdot\gamma \le40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma\qquad(0\le\lambda\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.* *These data cannot coexist.
  - Explanation: The fixed flag, jet system, intersection bounds, and movable class are incompatible by comparison of determinant vanishing orders.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-positive-characteristic-jets)): For a sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$, set $$N_p=\lfloor p/k_0\rfloor,\qquad B_p=k_0N_pD+T_0.$$ The sections of $pq\xi+B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}$ generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0N_p$ at $z_*$. In particular, they surject onto the quotient of its local ring by the $p$-th powers of all regular parameters.
  - Explanation: Products of the fixed jet system and filler sections give ordinary jets sufficient to surject onto the Frobenius local quotient.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-generic-rank)): The map in <a href="#fr:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:two-slot-evaluation">[fr:two-slot-evaluation]</a> has generic rank $R$.
  - Explanation: Projection to the fibre coefficient supplied by weights divisible by the residual characteristic proves generic full rank.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-truncated-slope)): There is a constant $c\ge0$, independent of the sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$ and of $j$, such that for $V=\Omega_W^1\|_C$ one has $$\mu_{\max}(A_j(V))\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.$$
  - Explanation: Complementary truncated symmetric degrees and uniform Frobenius slope control bound the maximal slope independently of the filtration index.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-diagonal-sections)): For all sufficiently large $p$, $$h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p) \le R\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr)<R/4.$$ The error constant is independent of the filtration index $j$.
  - Explanation: A common flag polynomial bounds every graded piece, so summing ranks controls sections on the Frobenius diagonal.
- **C25** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-full-all)): Let $n\geq 2$, and assume $(P_j)$ for every $j<n$, together with Theorems <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> and <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a>. Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over $\mathbb C$ of dimension $n$, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. Suppose that $T=K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective and that, for some rational $a>0$, $$(T+aM)\cdot C>0$$ for every integral curve $C$ through a very general point of $X$. Then $T+cM$ is big for every rational $c>0$.
  - Explanation: Index-one covers, terminalization, and boundary perturbation complete the full-dimension induction under its stated inputs.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-part)): For every projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair $(X,B)$ over $\mathbb C$ with $K_X+B$ pseudo-effective and every nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M$ on $X$, there is a smooth projective birational model $w\colon W\to X$ such that $$P_\sigma\bigl(w^*(K_X+B+M)\bigr)$$ is rational and numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-divisor.
  - Explanation: The positive part on a smooth birational model is rational and has a semiample numerical representative.
- **C27** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-positive-properties)): The following assertions hold.* 1. *Let $p\colon V\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a smooth projective variety to a normal projective variety. If $D$ is a pseudo-effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$ and $E$ is an effective $p$-exceptional rational divisor, then $$P_\sigma(p^*D+E)=P_\sigma(p^*D).$$* 2. *Let $f\colon V\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, and let $D$ be a pseudo-effective rational divisor on $S$. If $P_\sigma(D)$ is nef, then $$P_\sigma(f^*D)=f^*P_\sigma(D).$$* 3. *For rational divisors on a fixed smooth projective variety, rationality and numerical semiampleness of the positive part depend only on the numerical class of the divisor.
  - Explanation: Positive parts are invariant under effective exceptional additions and have the stated nef pullback and numerical-class properties.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, with $T=K_X+B$ pseudo-effective, and let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor on $X$. If $m$ is an integer greater than $2n$, there is a terminating $(T+mL)$-MMP whose every step is $L$-trivial and is an ordinary $T$-negative step. On its output, the transform of $L$ is Cartier and nef, and the transform of $T+mL$ is nef.
  - Explanation: Integrality of nef Cartier degrees forces the large-adjoint program to be trivial on the nef divisor.
- **C29** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-numsemiample-descent)): Let $p\colon W\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a smooth projective variety to a normal projective variety with rational singularities, over $\mathbb C$. Let $D$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If $p^*D$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-divisor on $W$, then $D$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$.
  - Explanation: Rational singularities permit a numerically trivial line-bundle twist to descend, carrying a semiample numerical representative downstairs.
- **C30** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-field-transfer)): Let $k_0\subset K$ be an extension of algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. Let $X_0$ be a normal projective variety over $k_0$, and let $D_0$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. If its pullback $D_K$ to $X_K$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, then $D_0$ has that property over $k_0$.
  - Explanation: Constructibility of global generation in the Picard family descends numerical semiampleness along algebraically closed field extensions.

          "source_id": "PM-036.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-input-models",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,B+\\mathbf M)$ be a projective generalized klt $\\mathbb Q$-pair over $\\mathbb C$, with $X$ $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and with fixed nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. If $K_X+B+M_X$ is pseudo-effective, there is a terminating choice of its minimal-model program, with $\\mathbb Q$-factorial generalized klt models and a nef endpoint. The program extracts no divisors and generalized log discrepancies do not decrease. On a common resolution of its initial and final models, the pullback of the initial adjoint is the pullback of the final adjoint plus an effective divisor exceptional over the final model.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,\\Delta+\\mathbf N)$ be a projective generalized klt $\\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, with effective boundary, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial $V$, and nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. Every $(K_V+\\Delta+N_V)$-negative extremal ray has an integral rational curve generator $C$ for which $$-4n\\leq (K_V+\\Delta+N_V)\\cdot C<0.$$ The contraction, and the flip when it is small, can be realized using an ordinary klt adjoint negative on the same ray. Birational MMP outputs remain $\\mathbb Q$-factorial. On a common resolution of a birational step, the pullback of the input generalized adjoint equals the pullback of its output transform plus an effective divisor exceptional over the output.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound",

Birational MMP outputs remain $\\mathbb Q$-factorial. On a common resolution of a birational step, the pullback of the input generalized adjoint equals the pullback of its output transform plus an effective divisor exceptional over the output.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-nef-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,B)$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial klt $\\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, let $T=K_X+B$ be pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. Suppose <a href=\"#eq:full-condition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:full-condition\">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds. Then there are a birational contraction $X\\dashrightarrow Y$, a number $s_0\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}$, and a common smooth projective resolution $$X\\xleftarrow{p}W\\xrightarrow{q}Y$$ with the following properties. If $B_Y$ and $N$ denote the transforms of $B$ and $M$, and $U=K_Y+B_Y$, then*\n\n1.  *$(Y,B_Y)$ is klt and $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, $U$ is semiample, and $\\kappa(Y,U)=\\kappa(X,T)\\geq0$;*\n\n2.  *$N$ is pseudo-effective, and $U+s_0N$ is nef of full nef dimension;*\n\n\n3.  *there are effective $q$-exceptional rational divisors $E_0,E_1$ on $W$ such that $$\\label{eq:two-comparisons}\n     p^*T=q^*U+E_0,\n     \\qquad\n     p^*(T+s_0M)=q^*(U+s_0N)+E_1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-bounded-curves",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $f\\geq1$ there is an integer $m_f>0$ with the following property. Let $Y$ be a smooth integral projective variety of dimension $f$, let $D_0$ be a reduced simple normal crossing divisor or the zero divisor, and let $L_0$ be a nef integral Cartier divisor of full nef dimension on $Y$. Suppose that $P_0$ is a nef rational Cartier divisor and that $r,C_0,C_1$ are positive real numbers satisfying $$0<P_0^f\\leq C_0,\\qquad P_0-rL_0\\text{ is nef}.$$ If $A_0=K_Y+D_0+L_0$ is big and $$\\label{j:adjoint-ratio}\n A_0\\cdot P_0^{f-1}\\leq C_1P_0^f,$$ then $$\\label{j:bounded-curve-obstruction}\n r\\leq(m_fC_1)^{f-1}C_0.$$ The integer $m_f$ depends only on $f$, and not on the variety or the divisors. For dimensions $1\\leq f\\leq n$, one may replace all $m_f$ by a single integer $m$ depending only on $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-bounded-curves",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $f\\geq1$ there is an integer $m_f>0$ with the following property. For dimensions $1\\leq f\\leq n$, one may replace all $m_f$ by a single integer $m$ depending only on $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-very-general-images",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a normal integral projective variety that is not uniruled, and let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor of full nef dimension on $X$. There is a complement $X^\\circ$ of a countable union of proper closed subsets of $X$ with the following property. If $Y$ is a smooth integral projective variety and $g:Y\\to X$ is generically finite onto a positive-dimensional image meeting $X^\\circ$, then $g^*L$ has full nef dimension, $Y$ is not uniruled, and $K_Y$ is pseudo-effective.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-j-very-general-images"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-subadjunction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety, let $\\Theta\\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\\subset Z$ be integral of dimension $f>0$. Suppose that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is log canonical at the generic point of $V$ and has a divisor of log discrepancy zero with center $V$. If $P$ is a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$ and $\\rho:V^*\\to V$ is a smooth projective resolution factoring through its normalization, then $$K_{V^*}\\cdot(\\rho^*P)^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+\\Theta)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-base-component",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $R$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer such that $kR$ is Cartier. Let $0\\ne\\mathcal H\\subset H^0(Z,\\mathcal O_Z(kR))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\\eta_V$, and that $\\eta_V\\in Z_{\\mathrm{sm}}$. Let $\\mathfrak m$ be the maximal ideal of $\\mathcal O_{Z,\\eta_V}$. If $\\mathfrak b\\mathcal O_{Z,\\eta_V}\\subset\\mathfrak m^h$ for an integer $h>0$, there are a rational number $0<c\\leq(d-f)k/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\\Theta\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}cR$ such that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is log canonical at $\\eta_V$ and has a log canonical place centered on $V$. The degree and canonical bounds are $$\\label{j:base-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n R^f\\cdot V&\\leq(k/h)^{d-f}R^d,\\\\\n K_{V^*}\\cdot R^{f-1}\n   &\\leq(K_Z+cR)|_V\\cdot R^{f-1}\\qquad(f>0),\n \\end{split}$$ where $V^*$ is a smooth projective resolution. The degree bound also holds for $f=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-moving-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, let $R$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $k>0$ such that $kR$ is Cartier. Choose positive real numbers $\\tau_0<\\cdots<\\tau_d$ with consecutive gap $\\delta>0$. For $x\\in Z_{\\mathrm{sm}}$, put $$\\mathcal H_i(x)=H^0\\bigl(Z,\\mathcal O_Z(kR)\n                  \\otimes\\mathfrak m_x^{\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil}\\bigr).$$ Suppose that for very general $x$ all these spaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an integral parameter variety $T$ with a dominant marked-point map $x:T\\to Z_{\\mathrm{sm}}$, a fixed index $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,d-1\\}$, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ containing $x(t)$ and common to the base loci of $\\mathcal H_i(x(t))$ and $\\mathcal H_{i+1}(x(t))$. In particular, the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$.*\n\n*For general $t$, the dimension $f$ of $V_t$ lies in $[1,d-1]$, $V_t$ is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and the entire higher-kernel base ideal is contained there in the ordinary power $\\mathcal I_{V_t}^h$, where $$h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ The incidence base ideal is likewise contained in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. These containments are taken in the smooth ambient open. If $Z$ is also $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt, then $$\\label{j:moving-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n R^f\\cdot V_t&\\leq(2/\\delta)^{d-f}R^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}\\cdot R^{f-1}\n   &\\leq(K_Z+cR)|_{V_t}\\cdot R^{f-1},\n       \\qquad 0<c\\leq2(d-f)/\\delta.\n \\end{split}$$ The top intersection on $V_t$ is positive when $V_t$ meets the open on which the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-j-moving-kernel",

These containments are taken in the smooth ambient open. If $Z$ is also $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt, then $$\\label{j:moving-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n R^f\\cdot V_t&\\leq(2/\\delta)^{d-f}R^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}\\cdot R^{f-1}\n   &\\leq(K_Z+cR)|_{V_t}\\cdot R^{f-1},\n       \\qquad 0<c\\leq2(d-f)/\\delta.\n \\end{split}$$ The top intersection on $V_t$ is positive when $V_t$ meets the open on which the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-fiber-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#j:moving-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"j:moving-kernel\">14</a>, let $g:Z\\to B$ be a morphism. There is a dense open of the incidence with the following property. Choose $(t,z)$ in this open, put $b=g(z)$, and suppose that $V_t$ is smooth over a smooth open of its actual image at $z$. Let $F$ be the reduced component through $z$ of the scheme fiber of $V_t\\to g(V_t)$. Near $z$ in $Z_b$, the restricted higher-kernel base ideal has support exactly $F$ and is contained in the ordinary power $\\mathcal I_F^h$.*\n\n*If $F$ is proper in an integral ambient fiber component containing it, the restricted series is nonzero there and $F$ is an isolated base component at its generic point. Applying Lemma <a href=\"#j:base-component\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"j:base-component\">13</a> on that ambient component additionally requires its projectivity, $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality, klt singularities, and smoothness at the generic point of $F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-j-finite-covers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal integral projective variety, let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism. Suppose that the general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$.*\n\n*After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\\mathrm{sm}}$ to divisors $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ in $\\mathcal V$. Retain this notation after the base change. Let $I$ consist of the indices $j$ for which $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume that $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ After shrinking again, there is a smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ over which every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ is finite étale. Only finitely many such normal covers of $Y$ occur, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections; the open and the finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-j-finite-covers",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal integral projective variety, let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism. Suppose that the general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$.*\n\n*After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\\mathrm{sm}}$ to divisors $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ in $\\mathcal V$. Retain this notation after the base change. Let $I$ consist of the indices $j$ for which $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume that $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ After shrinking again, there is a smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ over which every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ is finite étale. Only finitely many such normal covers of $Y$ occur, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections; the open and the finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Y$ be a normal integral projective variety, let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism. Suppose that the general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$.*\n\n*After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\\mathrm{sm}}$ to divisors $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ in $\\mathcal V$. Retain this notation after the base change. Let $I$ consist of the indices $j$ for which $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume that $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ After shrinking again, there is a smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ over which every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ is finite étale. Only finitely many such normal covers of $Y$ occur, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections; the open and the finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.",
      "explanation": "Non-sweeping relative branch images supply a fixed étale open and finiteness of bounded-degree normal covers.",

          "source_id": "PM-036.2"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal integral projective variety, let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism. Retain this notation after the base change. Let $I$ consist of the indices $j$ for which $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume that $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ After shrinking again, there is a smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ over which every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ is finite étale. Only finitely many such normal covers of $Y$ occur, up to isomorphism over $Y$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-large-seshadri",
        "exact_quote": "For a suitable fixed positive integer $q$, and all sufficiently small $t$ in <a href=\"#eq:scaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scaling\">[eq:scaling]</a>, there is a smooth point $z\\in Z$ in the torus open, lying over smooth points of both factors, such that $$\\label{j:large-seshadri}\n                         \\varepsilon(R;z)>2n+2.$$ More generally, for any prescribed dense open subsets $U_1,U_2$ of the smooth locus of $X$, the point may be chosen over $U_1\\times U_2$. In particular, these opens may be the isomorphism loci of fixed resolutions of $X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$. Let $D,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\\label{fr:intersection-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2D+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<\\tfrac14,\\qquad 80\\epsilon<\\tfrac34.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$ for which $l_iH$ is Cartier, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On the projective bundle $$Z=\\mathbb P\\bigl(\\mathcal O_W(S)_1\\oplus\n                             \\mathcal O_W(S)_2\\bigr)\\longrightarrow W\\times W,\n     \\qquad \\xi=c_1(\\mathcal O_Z(1)),\\qquad M=D_1+D_2+q\\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\\mathcal O_Z(a\\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum for $a\\ge0$; subscripts indicate pullback from the corresponding factor of $W\\times W$. The two axes are the sections defined by the two summands. A smooth point $z_*$ in their complement and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0D$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{fr:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-data",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$. Let $D,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\\label{fr:intersection-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2D+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<\\tfrac14,\\qquad 80\\epsilon<\\tfrac34.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$ for which $l_iH$ is Cartier, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On the projective bundle $$Z=\\mathbb P\\bigl(\\mathcal O_W(S)_1\\oplus\n                             \\mathcal O_W(S)_2\\bigr)\\longrightarrow W\\times W,\n     \\qquad \\xi=c_1(\\mathcal O_Z(1)),\\qquad M=D_1+D_2+q\\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\\mathcal O_Z(a\\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum for $a\\ge0$; subscripts indicate pullback from the corresponding factor of $W\\times W$. The two axes are the sections defined by the two summands. A smooth point $z_*$ in their complement and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0D$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{fr:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.2"
        }

The two axes are the sections defined by the two summands. A smooth point $z_*$ in their complement and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0D$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{fr:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",

        "exact_quote": "For a sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$, set $$N_p=\\lfloor p/k_0\\rfloor,\\qquad B_p=k_0N_pD+T_0.$$ The sections of $pq\\xi+B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}$ generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0N_p$ at $z_*$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **nef divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A nef divisor has nonnegative degree on every curve.
- **semiample divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A semiample divisor has a positive Cartier multiple generated by global sections, and therefore defines a morphism to a projective variety.
- **numerically semiample** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We call a rational Cartier divisor *numerically semiample* if it is numerically equivalent to a semiample rational Cartier divisor on the same variety.
- **numerical equivalence** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here numerical equivalence means equality of degrees on all integral curves.
- **rational pair** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A $\mathbb Q$-pair $(X,B)$ consists of a normal variety, an effective rational boundary $B$, and a rational Cartier divisor $K_X+B$.
- **klt and log canonical** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The pair is klt if all log discrepancies are positive, and log canonical if they are nonnegative.
- **pseudo-effective and big** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A divisor is pseudo-effective if its numerical class is in the closure of the cone generated by effective divisors, and big if its class lies in the interior of that cone.
- **very general point** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Over $\mathbb C$, a very general point means a point outside a specified countable union of proper closed subsets.
- **nef reduction** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a nef divisor $N$, its nef reduction is an almost holomorphic dominant rational map with connected compact general fibres, on which $N$ is numerically trivial, such that every curve through a very general point not contracted by the map has positive $N$-degree [[NefReduction](#ref-NefReduction)].
- **slope** ([source](#pm-h-27)): For a vector bundle on a smooth projective curve, its slope is degree divided by rank.
- **jet generation** ([source](#pm-h-27)): For a line bundle on a smooth variety, to generate jets through order $a$ at a point means surjectivity to its stalk modulo the $(a+1)$-st power of the maximal ideal.

          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-data",
            "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$. Let $D,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\\label{fr:intersection-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2D+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<\\tfrac14,\\qquad 80\\epsilon<\\tfrac34.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$ for which $l_iH$ is Cartier, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On the projective bundle $$Z=\\mathbb P\\bigl(\\mathcal O_W(S)_1\\oplus\n                             \\mathcal O_W(S)_2\\bigr)\\longrightarrow W\\times W,\n     \\qquad \\xi=c_1(\\mathcal O_Z(1)),\\qquad M=D_1+D_2+q\\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\\mathcal O_Z(a\\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum for $a\\ge0$; subscripts indicate pullback from the corresponding factor of $W\\times W$. The two axes are the sections defined by the two summands. A smooth point $z_*$ in their complement and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0D$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{fr:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lemma-two-nef-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-two-nef-model): Compatible terminating programs retain the semiample ordinary adjoint. -> A nearby full-nef-dimension generalized adjoint.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-full-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-full-positive): Induction on fibres of the ordinary semiample fibration. -> Bigness when the ordinary Iitaka dimension is positive.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-reduction-cy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reduction-cy): Separate positive and zero ordinary Iitaka dimension. -> Reduction to klt Calabi–Yau pairs.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lemma-bounded-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-bounded-curves): Polarized birationality, mixed-intersection inequalities, and integral curve degrees. -> A bounded rescaling parameter.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-thm-terminal-big) / [proof](#sec:geometry): Exclude moving components using bounded-degree curves, fixed covers, and an abelian model. -> Contrasting scalar and projective-bundle jet estimates.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-data) / [proof](#sec:frobenius): Spread fixed data and compare determinant orders using full rank, diagonal filtration, and a movable class. -> Incompatibility of the fixed complex data.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-terminal-big) / [proof](#fr:application): Pull back the scaled polarization, perturb it amply, and separate a non-pseudo-effective blowup class. -> Bigness in the terminal trivial-canonical case.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-full-all) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-full-all): Index-one cover and terminalization followed by a klt boundary perturbation. -> Full-dimension bigness for klt pairs.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-part) / [proof](#sec:positive): Run a nef-divisor-trivial program, then use bigness or descend along nef reduction and a relative good model. -> Rational numerically semiample positive part on a smooth model.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-44): Use nefness to eliminate the negative part and descend through rational singularities. -> A semiample numerical representative on the complex variety.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-45): Descend globally generated line-bundle twists through the Picard parameter space. -> The result over the stated algebraically closed characteristic-zero field.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-main**: A nef generalized adjoint has a semiample numerical representative when the ordinary adjoint is pseudo-effective and the nef summand is a rational Cartier divisor on the original variety.
- **I-cy**: Every nef rational Cartier divisor on a klt pair with numerically trivial ordinary adjoint is numerically semiample.
- **I-terminal**: Full nef dimension implies bigness for the specified terminal variety and Cartier divisor under the lower-dimensional induction hypotheses.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-linear**: Conclude that the original nef summand itself has a globally generated positive multiple. -> Numerical equivalence can discard a nontorsion degree-zero twist whose positive powers have no sections.
- **A-scope**: Apply the main theorem to arbitrary geometric or software data without proving its geometric premises. -> The conclusion lacks a justified application outside the stated singularity, field, divisor, and positivity regime.
- **A-covers**: Treat a bound on cover degree as a finite enumeration of all branched covers. -> Moving branch divisors can prevent a fixed branch complement and hence prevent the stated finiteness conclusion.
- **A-termination**: Infer termination of every possible sequence of flips from the minimal-model input. -> Existence of a terminating choice does not certify arbitrary choices of program.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main conclusion supplies a semiample numerical representative on a projective klt rational pair in characteristic zero. It does not specify a finite construction of that representative or a decision procedure for the geometric premises. The proof uses very general points, sufficiently small parameters, and sufficiently large residual characteristic; these choices do not supply a finite executable input contract.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The main conclusion requires a projective klt rational pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, a pseudo-effective ordinary adjoint, a nef rational Cartier summand, and nefness of their sum. Blocked use: ['Applying the main theorem to arbitrary singular pairs, nonprojective varieties, irrational summands, or positive-characteristic varieties.', 'Omitting pseudo-effectivity or either nefness assumption.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The original nef summand is a divisor on the original variety; nef data retained on a higher birational model during the proof do not enlarge the main theorem to arbitrary generalized pairs. Blocked use: ['Replacing the descended nef summand by arbitrary nef b-divisor data.']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): Numerical semiampleness does not imply semiampleness of the given divisor: a nontorsion degree-zero line bundle on a complex elliptic curve supplies the stated obstruction. Blocked use: ['Claiming global sections or a morphism from a positive multiple of the given divisor solely from numerical semiampleness.']
- **L4** (nonconstructive): The main conclusion asserts existence of a semiample numerical representative. A finite construction algorithm, an input encoding, and computational complexity bounds for that representative are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Publishing an executable construction or a performance guarantee on the basis of this existence statement.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The proof uses companion log abundance and minimal-model results as inputs; the generalized nef-summand conclusion is not a conclusion of either input alone. Blocked use: ['Presenting the main theorem as independent of the companion inputs.']
- **L6** (edge_case): The minimal-model input requires existence of a terminating choice of program, not termination of every sequence of flips. Blocked use: ['Asserting termination for arbitrary choices of minimal-model steps.']
- **L7** (edge_case): Full nef dimension is a positivity condition on curves through very general points; the definition alone does not assert bigness. Blocked use: ['Equating full nef dimension with bigness outside the hypotheses of the proved geometric results.']
- **L8** (edge_case): The restriction of the isolated base ideal is made through a general incidence point with smoothness over the actual image. The same conclusion is not asserted for an arbitrary special fiber. Blocked use: ['Applying the restriction statement on arbitrary special fibers without its incidence and smoothness assumptions.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Bounded degree alone does not make branched covers finite. The argument requires non-sweeping branch images and a fixed smooth branch complement for the family. Blocked use: ['Inferring finiteness of branched covers merely from a bound on their degree.']
- **L10** (approximation): The finite cover lists and their branch complement may depend on the fixed scaling parameter; they need not be uniform as that parameter tends to zero. Blocked use: ['Using a single cover list uniformly across the scaling limit.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The Frobenius comparison fixes the flag, movable curve witness, jet sections, and complex geometric data before the residual characteristic tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Replacing fixed geometric data by characteristic-dependent choices without proving the required uniformity.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The main theorem is a mathematical specification candidate. A completed machine-checked proof, a finite decision procedure for its geometric predicates, and a direct software implementation bridge are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating a theorem statement as a formal certificate or an executable oracle for arbitrary software inputs.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Generalised Abundance Conjecture: if $`(X,B)`$ is a projective klt ℚ-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, $`K_X+B`$ is pseudo-effective, <i>M</i> is a nef ℚ-Cartier divisor on <i>X</i>, and $`K_X+B+M`$ is nef, then $`K_X+B+M`$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample ℚ-Cartier divisor on <i>X</i>.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A nef divisor has nonnegative degree on every curve. A semiample divisor has a positive Cartier multiple generated by global sections, and therefore defines a morphism to a projective variety. The passage from numerical positivity to such a morphism is central to the minimal model program. For an ordinary log canonical divisor, this is the abundance problem. Adding an arbitrary nef divisor changes the natural conclusion: one must allow replacement by a numerically equivalent divisor.

We call a rational Cartier divisor *numerically semiample* if it is numerically equivalent to a semiample rational Cartier divisor on the same variety. Here numerical equivalence means equality of degrees on all integral curves. We prove the following statement.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If $$D=K_X+B+M$$ is nef, then there is a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $X$ such that $D\equiv L$.*

</div>

The rationality assumptions apply to the boundary and the nef summand; in particular the latter is a divisor on $X$ itself. The proof uses the log-abundance theorem of <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Theorem 1.1] and the terminating minimal-model programs of <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[MM](#ref-MM), Theorem 1.1 and Section 3.3]. Their precise forms are stated in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>.

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> contains the Generalised Abundance Conjecture formulated by Lazić–Peternell <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP)], where the nef summand is Cartier. Numerical equivalence is necessary even in dimension one. Indeed, a nontorsion degree-zero line bundle on a complex elliptic curve is nef and numerically trivial, but none of its positive powers has a nonzero section. Since the canonical bundle of the curve is trivial, this is an example with $B=0$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For the ordinary adjoint, Fukuda’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Fukuda](#ref-Fukuda), Theorem 0.1] shows that a semiample numerical representative makes $K_X+B$ itself semiample. For $M=0$, the conclusion is therefore equivalent to ordinary klt abundance, which is an input to this proof.

<a id="stmt-cor-cy"></a>
<div id="cor:cy" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair in characteristic zero with $K_X+B\equiv0$. Every nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$ is numerically semiample.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cy"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cy</summary>

*Proof.* For a nef divisor $M$, the divisor $K_X+B+M$ is nef and numerically equivalent to $M$. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Background and the role of the present argument

The generalized-pair formalism retains a nef divisor on a higher birational model as part of the adjoint data. Birkar–Zhang <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BZ](#ref-BZ)] developed effective birationality for these polarized pairs, extending the tools used to study Iitaka fibrations. The canonical bundle formula of Ambro <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ambro](#ref-Ambro)] is one source of such nef data. In this paper the original nef summand descends to $X$, but a minimal-model program forces us to keep track of it as a fixed nef divisor on a higher model.

Lazić–Peternell <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP)] formulated generalized abundance as a numerical extension of abundance and related it to semiampleness on varieties with trivial canonical class. Their Theorem B, assuming termination of flips and abundance in dimensions at most $n$, proves numerical semiampleness when the ordinary adjoint has positive numerical dimension. Its numerical-dimension-zero case also assumes their Semiampleness Conjecture. Their Corollaries C and D give unconditional results for surfaces and for threefolds with ordinary adjoint of positive numerical dimension. These results identify the main remaining positivity issue: an arbitrary nef summand on a variety with trivial canonical class need not supply sections directly.

Subsequent work has addressed numerical nonvanishing, which asks for an effective numerical representative, and refinements of numerical semiampleness. Lazić–Peternell <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LPII](#ref-LPII), Theorems B and C] obtained numerical nonvanishing under additional numerical or metric hypotheses and, in the metric case, conjectural MMP and abundance assumptions. For a Cartier nef summand, Chaudhuri <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Chaudhuri](#ref-Chaudhuri), Corollary 3] proved generalized abundance when the nef-reduction base of $D$ has dimension two and $K_X+B$ is effective, or when that base has dimension three and $\kappa(K_X+B)>0$. Here nef reduction contracts the curves of degree zero through very general points; its precise properties are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>. On surfaces, Fontanari <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Fontanari](#ref-Fontanari), Theorem 1.4] proved actual semiampleness when the nef summand is Cartier and its numerical class is not proportional to that of the ordinary adjoint.

The birational reductions below follow <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Sections 4–7]. The companion results <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), [MM](#ref-MM)] supply ordinary log abundance and the existence of the particular terminating programs required in those reductions. The further ingredient proved here is that a nef divisor of full nef dimension on a klt Calabi–Yau pair is big. Full nef dimension means that every curve through a very general point has positive degree. Bigness is stronger: it requires the volume of the divisor to be positive. The gap between these two conditions is the geometric issue addressed by the proof.

Our treatment adapts the moving-jet and Frobenius arguments of <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Sections 7–9]. The moving-point method has antecedents in the local-positivity work of Ein–Küchle–Lazarsfeld <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[EKL](#ref-EKL)]. The finite-data comparison uses Frobenius jets, as studied by Mustaţă–Schwede <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[MustataSchwede](#ref-MustataSchwede)], the canonical filtrations of Sun and Kitadai–Sumihiro <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Sun](#ref-Sun), [KS](#ref-KS)], and Langer’s control of Frobenius slopes <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Langer](#ref-Langer)]. We reproduce the comparison proof with its uniformity conditions. The adaptation to an arbitrary nef summand occurs in the geometric exclusions preceding it: the polarized birationality theorem of Birkar–Zhang gives curves of bounded degree, contradicting a growing integral lower bound for their degrees. We give explicit proofs of those exclusions, including the case of correspondences between finite covers.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Structure of the proof

There are two simultaneous induction statements. The first says that $K_X+B+cM$ is big for every rational $c>0$ whenever one positive combination of $K_X+B$ and $M$ has positive degree on all curves through a very general point. The second says that the positive part of $K_X+B+M$ on a suitable smooth birational model is rational and numerically semiample. The positive part is the divisor left after removing the asymptotically fixed prime divisors. When the original adjoint is nef, it has no such negative part, and the second assertion descends to Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">3</a> sets up this induction. A pair of compatible minimal-model programs retains both a semiample ordinary adjoint and a nearby nef generalized adjoint. Positive Iitaka dimension then permits induction on the fibres. The remaining full-dimension case is a klt Calabi–Yau pair.

Sections <a href="#sec:jet-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jet-tools">4</a> and <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">5</a> treat the essential terminal case with trivial canonical bundle. If a nef Cartier divisor $L$ of full nef dimension were not big, its ample approximations could be rescaled to bounded volume while their degrees on very general curves tend to infinity. The bounded-degree obstruction controls the base loci of moving jet kernels. It produces a small vanishing-order bound on the variety itself and a large jet system on a projective bundle over its square. Section <a href="#sec:frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frobenius">6</a> shows that these two conclusions are incompatible. The comparison is made on fixed complex data before reduction to positive characteristic.

Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">7</a> passes through an index-one cover and a boundary perturbation to obtain the full-dimension assertion for all klt Calabi–Yau pairs. Section <a href="#sec:positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positive">8</a> proves the positive-part assertion by nef reduction and the canonical bundle formula, then descends numerical semiampleness to the original variety and to any algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Conventions and birational inputs

Varieties are integral and projective over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero unless stated otherwise. A $\mathbb Q$-pair $(X,B)$ consists of a normal variety, an effective rational boundary $B$, and a rational Cartier divisor $K_X+B$. We use log discrepancies: on a resolution $h:Y\to X$, the equality $K_Y+B_Y=h^*(K_X+B)$ assigns log discrepancy $1-b_E$ to a prime divisor with coefficient $b_E$ in $B_Y$. The pair is klt if all log discrepancies are positive, and log canonical if they are nonnegative. We use the standard cone, contraction, negativity, and resolution theorems as in <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KM](#ref-KM), [BCHM](#ref-BCHM)].

The symbols $\sim_\mathbb Q$ and $\equiv$ denote rational linear and numerical equivalence. A divisor is pseudo-effective if its numerical class is in the closure of the cone generated by effective divisors, and big if its class lies in the interior of that cone. Its Iitaka dimension is denoted by $\kappa$. Over $\mathbb C$, a very general point means a point outside a specified countable union of proper closed subsets.

For a nef divisor $N$, its nef reduction is an almost holomorphic dominant rational map with connected compact general fibres, on which $N$ is numerically trivial, such that every curve through a very general point not contracted by the map has positive $N$-degree <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[NefReduction](#ref-NefReduction)]. Its base dimension is the *nef dimension* $n(X,N)$. Thus $n(X,N)=\dim X$ precisely when every curve through a very general point has positive $N$-degree. This numerical condition does not assert bigness.

For a pseudo-effective real divisor $D$ on a smooth variety and a prime divisor $\Gamma$, fix an ample divisor $A$ and set $$\sigma_\Gamma(D)=\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}
 \inf\{\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_\Gamma D':D'\ge0,\ D'\sim_\mathbb RD+\delta A\}.$$ These numbers are independent of $A$ and depend only on the numerical class of $D$. The divisorial Zariski decomposition is $$N_\sigma(D)=\sum_\Gamma\sigma_\Gamma(D)\Gamma,
 \qquad P_\sigma(D)=D-N_\sigma(D).$$ The sum has finite support. A nef divisor has $N_\sigma(D)=0$. Further properties from <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Nakayama](#ref-Nakayama), [LP](#ref-LP)] are recorded at their point of use in Section <a href="#sec:positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positive">8</a>.

<a id="stmt-lemma-pseudo-vg"></a>
<div id="lemma:pseudo-vg" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Let $D$ be a pseudo-effective rational Cartier divisor on a projective complex variety $X$. Every integral curve through a sufficiently very general point has nonnegative $D$-degree.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-pseudo-vg"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-pseudo-vg</summary>

*Proof.* Fix an ample Cartier divisor $A$ and positive rational numbers $\delta_i\to0$. Each $D+\delta_iA$ is big, so there is an effective rational divisor $E_i\sim_\mathbb QD+\delta_iA$. Choose a point outside $\bigcup_i\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}E_i$. A curve through it is contained in none of these supports; hence $(D+\delta_iA)\cdot C\ge0$ for every $i$. Let $i\to\infty$. ◻

</details>

We state explicitly the companion results used throughout. We require existence of a terminating choice of program, rather than termination of every sequence of flips.

<a id="stmt-input-abundance"></a>
<div id="input:abundance" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Log abundance, <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Theorem 1.1]). *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective log canonical $\mathbb Q$-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. If $K_X+B$ is nef, then it is semiample.*

</div>

For the next input a nef rational Cartier b-divisor is specified by a nef rational Cartier divisor on a projective birational model; it is pulled back unchanged on all higher models. Its trace on a lower model is its pushforward and need not be nef. Generalized discrepancies are defined by pulling back the adjoint while keeping this fixed nef data.

<a id="stmt-input-models"></a>
<div id="input:models" class="theorem">

**Theorem 5** (Minimal-model programs, <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[MM](#ref-MM), Theorem 1.1 and Section 3.3]). *Let $(X,B+\mathbf M)$ be a projective generalized klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over $\mathbb C$, with $X$ $\mathbb Q$-factorial and with fixed nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. If $K_X+B+M_X$ is pseudo-effective, there is a terminating choice of its minimal-model program, with $\mathbb Q$-factorial generalized klt models and a nef endpoint. The program extracts no divisors and generalized log discrepancies do not decrease. On a common resolution of its initial and final models, the pullback of the initial adjoint is the pullback of the final adjoint plus an effective divisor exceptional over the final model.*

</div>

The ordinary klt case follows by taking the nef data to be zero. In that case Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> makes the endpoint a good minimal model. The moving-center and Frobenius results used from <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA)] are stated separately in Sections <a href="#sec:jet-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jet-tools">4</a> and <a href="#sec:frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frobenius">6</a>; neither theorem above asserts abundance for a nonzero generalized nef summand.

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### Reduction of full nef dimension to Calabi–Yau pairs

We work over $\mathbb C$ until the final descent argument. The reductions in this section follow the strategy of Lazić–Peternell <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Sections 4 and 5]. We use the terminating programs in Theorem <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a>, together with the abundance input Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a>, to make the precise reductions needed below. The essential point is to retain control of two adjoints: an ordinary semiample adjoint and a nearby generalized nef adjoint.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The induction statements

<a id="eq:full-condition"></a>
For a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair $(X,B)$ and a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M$ on $X$, write $$T=K_X+B.$$ Suppose that $T$ is pseudo-effective. We will use the condition that, for some $a\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$, $$\label{eq:full-condition}
 (T+aM)\cdot C>0
 \quad\text{for every integral curve $C$ through a very general point of $X$.}$$ The divisor $T+aM$ need not be nef. By Lemma <a href="#lemma:pseudo-vg" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:pseudo-vg">3</a>, Condition <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds whenever $M$ has full nef dimension. When $T+aM$ is nef, the condition says exactly that its nef dimension is $\dim X$.

We prove the following two assertions together, by induction on $n$. The notation $P_\sigma$ denotes the positive part of the divisorial Zariski decomposition; its properties used in the proof are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positive">8</a>.

$(P_n)$  
If $(X,B)$ is a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, $T=K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, $M$ is a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$, and <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds, then $T+cM$ is big for every $c\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$.

$(Z_n)$  
If $(X,B)$ and $M$ have the same hypotheses, without <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a>, then there is a projective birational morphism $w\colon W\to X$ from a smooth projective variety such that $$P_\sigma\bigl(w^*(T+M)\bigr)$$ is a rational divisor numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-divisor on $W$.

Both assertions hold in dimensions zero and one. In dimension one, pseudo-effectivity of $T$ and nefness of $M$ mean that their degrees are nonnegative. Condition <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> makes every positive combination have positive degree, hence be ample. A divisor of degree zero is numerically equivalent to zero, and a divisor of positive degree is ample. These observations also give $(Z_1)$, since a nef divisor has zero negative part. On a point the assertions use the usual conventions.

Fix $n\geq2$, and assume $(P_j)$ and $(Z_j)$ for $j<n$. At any stage we may replace $X$ by a small projective $\mathbb Q$-factorialization. The ordinary adjoint pulls back crepantly, $M$ pulls back to a nef divisor, and <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> persists by using points in the isomorphism locus. Bigness descends under this birational pullback. A smooth model proving $(Z_n)$ for the $\mathbb Q$-factorialization also proves it for $X$. We therefore impose $\mathbb Q$-factoriality when running the programs below.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Keeping a semiample adjoint fixed

We first record the elementary ray comparison used in the second program. The nef part of a generalized pair in this argument is the fixed nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier b-divisor determined by $M$ on the original variety. Its trace on a later model need not be nef.

<a id="stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound"></a>
<div id="lemma:generalized-ray-bound" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $(V,\Delta+\mathbf N)$ be a projective generalized klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, with effective boundary, $\mathbb Q$-factorial $V$, and nef rational Cartier b-divisor data. Every $(K_V+\Delta+N_V)$-negative extremal ray has an integral rational curve generator $C$ for which $$-4n\leq (K_V+\Delta+N_V)\cdot C<0.$$ The contraction, and the flip when it is small, can be realized using an ordinary klt adjoint negative on the same ray. Birational MMP outputs remain $\mathbb Q$-factorial. On a common resolution of a birational step, the pullback of the input generalized adjoint equals the pullback of its output transform plus an effective divisor exceptional over the output.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-generalized-ray-bound</summary>

*Proof.* Set $G=K_V+\Delta+N_V$, and fix a $G$-negative extremal ray. For an ample rational divisor $A$ on $V$, choose $\eta>0$ rational and small enough that $G+\eta A$ is still negative on the ray and $$-(G+\eta A)\cdot\gamma
                      \geq -\tfrac12G\cdot\gamma$$ for its nonzero classes $\gamma$. We may represent $G+\eta A$ as an ordinary klt adjoint. Here is the reason this remains valid when the nef b-divisor does not descend to $V$.

Choose a projective log resolution $h\colon V'\to V$ carrying its nef part $N'$ and write $$K_{V'}+\Delta'+N'=h^*G.$$ The coefficients of $\Delta'$ are strictly less than one. The resolution can be chosen with an effective exceptional divisor $F$ such that $-F$ is $h$-ample. For sufficiently small rational $\varepsilon>0$, the divisor $N'+\eta h^*A-\varepsilon F$ is ample. A general effective rational representative of this ample class can be chosen with sufficiently small coefficients that, after adding $\varepsilon F$, it preserves the sub-klt condition of $\Delta'$. Its pushforward, added to $\Delta$, is effective and defines a klt boundary $\Theta$ on $V$ with $$K_V+\Theta\sim_\mathbb QG+\eta A.$$ The crepant equality can be checked upstairs: its difference is exceptional and relatively numerically trivial, hence zero by negativity.

The ordinary cone theorem now gives a rational curve generator $C$ with $0<-(G+\eta A)\cdot C\leq2n$. The preceding comparison yields $-G\cdot C\leq4n$. The contraction $c\colon V\to S$ is the contraction of the same ray for this ordinary klt pair. Since its relative Picard number is one, there is a rational $\lambda>0$ such that $G-\lambda(K_V+\Theta)$ is numerically trivial over $S$. The Cartier descent statement for an ordinary klt extremal contraction, after clearing denominators, gives a rational Cartier divisor $D_S$ with $$G-\lambda(K_V+\Theta)\sim_\mathbb Qc^*D_S.$$ In the small case, let $c^+\colon V^+\to S$ be the ordinary flip. Taking strict transforms gives $$G^+\sim_\mathbb Q
