We bound the sections of every graded bundle in <a href="#fr:diagonal-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:diagonal-graded">[fr:diagonal-graded]</a> by its rank times the same scalar polynomial in $p$. This uniformity lets us sum the ranks of the graded pieces without an additional factor for their number. We begin with a slope bound on the fixed curve; here slope means ordinary degree divided by rank.

<a id="stmt-fr-truncated-slope"></a>
<div id="fr:truncated-slope" class="lemma">

**Lemma 23**. *There is a constant $c\ge0$, independent of the sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$ and of $j$, such that for $V=\Omega_W^1|_C$ one has $$\mu_{\max}(A_j(V))\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fr-truncated-slope"><summary>Proof of stmt-fr-truncated-slope</summary>

*Proof.* Write $F_C$ for absolute Frobenius of $C$. Langer’s instability estimate <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[Langer](#ref-Langer), Corollary 2.5] gives <a id="fr:Langer-min"></a>$$\label{fr:Langer-min}
 L_{\min}(V):=
 \lim_{e\to\infty}p^{-e}\mu_{\min}(F_C^{e*}V)
 \ge-\frac{c}{p-1}.$$ To make the uniformity explicit, let $g$ be the fixed genus of $C$. One can choose a nef line bundle $A_C$ of degree at most $d_g=\max\{4g-2,0\}$ such that $T_C\otimes A_C$ is globally generated; for $g\le1$, take $A_C=\mathcal O_C$. Langer’s bound is $$\mu_{\min}(V)-L_{\min}(V)
 \le\frac{(\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}V-1)\deg A_C}{p-1}.$$ Since $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}V=n$ and $\mu_{\min}(V)\ge0$, we may take $c=(n-1)d_g$, independently of the reduction.

<a id="fr:tensor-min"></a>
We also need, for every integer $i\ge0$, $$\label{fr:tensor-min}
 \mu_{\min}(V^{\otimes i})\ge iL_{\min}(V).$$ Fix $p$ and $i$. Choose a line bundle $A_e$ of degree $-\mu_{\min}(F_C^{e*}V)+O(1)$, rounded up with a fixed genus-dependent additive constant, so that $F_C^{e*}V\otimes A_e$ is globally generated. This follows from Serre duality: after subtracting any point, its minimum slope can be made greater than $2g(C)-2$, which annihilates $H^1$ and makes evaluation at that point surjective. For every vector-bundle quotient $Q$ of $V^{\otimes i}$, the bundle $F_C^{e*}Q\otimes A_e^{\otimes i}$ is then globally generated. Consequently $$p^e\mu(Q)+i\deg A_e\ge0.$$ Divide by $p^e$ and let $e\to\infty$ to prove <a href="#fr:tensor-min" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:tensor-min">[fr:tensor-min]</a>. This limit is taken for each fixed $p,i$; there is no interchange of Frobenius-iteration and characteristic limits.

Since $A_{u-j}(V)$ is a quotient of $V^{\otimes(u-j)}$, the perfect pairing <a href="#fr:socle-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:socle-pairing">[fr:socle-pairing]</a> yields $$\begin{aligned}
 \mu_{\max}(A_j(V))
 &= (p-1)\deg\det V-\mu_{\min}(A_{u-j}(V))\\
 &\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+\frac{(u-j)c}{p-1}\\
 &\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.
\end{aligned}$$ ◻

</details>

The divisors $B_p-pD=T_0-(p-k_0N_p)D$ range over a fixed finite list. Combining <a href="#fr:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:intersection-bounds">[fr:intersection-bounds]</a>, <a href="#fr:flag-degrees" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:flag-degrees">[fr:flag-degrees]</a>, and Lemma <a href="#fr:truncated-slope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fr:truncated-slope">23</a> therefore gives, uniformly for $0\le j\le u$, <a id="fr:common-slope-bound"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mu_{\max}(\mathcal G_j|_C)
 &\le p(4+2/r)\Lambda H^n+O(1)\notag\\
 &\le M_p:=7p\Lambda H^n+c_0,\label{fr:common-slope-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ where $c_0\ge0$ is fixed. The coefficient $7$ provides harmless room in this common bound.

<a id="stmt-fr-diagonal-sections"></a>
<div id="fr:diagonal-sections" class="lemma">

**Lemma 24**. *For all sufficiently large $p$, $$h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p)
 \le R\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr)<R/4.$$ The error constant is independent of the filtration index $j$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-fr-diagonal-sections"><summary>Proof of stmt-fr-diagonal-sections</summary>

<a id="fr:flag-lattice-sum"></a>
*Proof.* Write the fixed flag as $$W=W_n\supset W_{n-1}\supset\cdots\supset W_1=C,
 \qquad W_{n-k}\in|h_k|_{W_{n-k+1}}
 \quad(1\le k\le n-1).$$ At step $k$, fix the preceding nonnegative integers $b_1,\ldots,b_{k-1}$ and put $$\mathcal F_k=
 \mathcal G_j|_{W_{n-k+1}}
 \Bigl(-\sum_{i<k}b_i h_i\Bigr).$$ For each $b_k\ge0$, the divisor restriction sequence is $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal F_k(-(b_k+1)h_k)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal F_k(-b_kh_k)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathcal F_k(-b_kh_k)|_{W_{n-k}}
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Iterating it for $b_k=0,1,\ldots$ and then proceeding to the next member of the flag bounds the section space by $$\label{fr:flag-lattice-sum}
 h^0(W,\mathcal G_j)\le
 \sum_{b_1,\ldots,b_{n-1}\ge0}
 h^0\left(C,\mathcal G_j|_C\Bigl(-\sum_i b_i h_i|_C\Bigr)\right).$$ At each stage the remainder is zero after sufficiently negative ample twisting. Its stopping index need not be uniform in $j,p$ or in the preceding twists: enlarging the nonnegative finite sums to the displayed lattice sum preserves the inequality.

<a id="fr:common-flag-polynomial"></a>
A summand in <a href="#fr:flag-lattice-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:flag-lattice-sum">[fr:flag-lattice-sum]</a> vanishes when $\sum_i b_i(h_i\cdot C)>M_p$, because then its maximum slope is negative. Each nonzero summand has at most $e_j(M_p+1)$ sections. Indeed, evaluation at $\lfloor M_p\rfloor+1$ distinct points is injective; the kernel has negative maximum slope after subtracting those points. By <a href="#fr:flag-degrees" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:flag-degrees">[fr:flag-degrees]</a> it follows that $$\begin{aligned}
 h^0(W,\mathcal G_j)
 &\le e_j(M_p+1)
       \prod_{i=1}^{n-1}
       \left(1+\frac{M_p}{l_i\Lambda H^n}\right)\notag\\
 &=e_jp^n\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr).
 \label{fr:common-flag-polynomial}
\end{aligned}$$ The leading coefficient follows from $\Lambda=\prod_i l_i$. More importantly, the whole scalar polynomial on the first line is the same for every $j$.

Sum over the filtration in <a href="#fr:diagonal-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:diagonal-graded">[fr:diagonal-graded]</a>. The ranks satisfy $\sum_j e_j=p^n$, so <a href="#fr:common-flag-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:common-flag-polynomial">[fr:common-flag-polynomial]</a> gives the asserted bound with $R=p^{2n}$. There is no extra factor for the number of graded pieces. Finally $$7^nH^n\le(14\epsilon)^n<1/4$$ by <a href="#fr:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:intersection-bounds">[fr:intersection-bounds]</a>, proving the strict inequality for all large $p$. ◻

</details>

<a id="fr:determinant"></a>
#### The two orders of the determinant

Choose $R$ generically independent columns of <a href="#fr:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:two-slot-evaluation">[fr:two-slot-evaluation]</a> from bases of its source vector spaces. Their determinant is a nonzero section $$0\ne\sigma\in H^0(W'\times W',\mathcal Q_1\boxtimes\mathcal Q_2)$$ of an actual external-product line bundle. To specify its factors, let $m_i$ be the sum of the weights of the $R$ chosen columns in slot $i$. The target determinant is $(\det\mathcal E)^s\boxtimes(\det\mathcal E)^s$, and the chosen source determinant is $\mathcal O(-m_1S')\boxtimes\mathcal O(-m_2S')$. Thus $$\mathcal Q_i=(\det\mathcal E)^{\otimes s}
                 \otimes\mathcal O_{W'}(m_iS'),\qquad
 \lambda_i=m_i/R.$$ Every column weight lies between $0$ and $q$, so $0\le\lambda_i\le q$ independently of the columns chosen.

<a id="fr:slot-determinant-class"></a>
Under the numerical identification with the base-field twist, $$\label{fr:slot-determinant-class}
 \frac{c_1(\mathcal Q_i)}R
 =\frac{c_1(\mathcal E)}s+\lambda_iS
 =\frac{B_p}p+\frac{p-1}{2p}K_W+\lambda_iS,
 \qquad 0\le\lambda_i\le q.$$ The Frobenius first-Chern-class identity is also <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[Sun](#ref-Sun), Lemma 4.2]; it is an identity of rational divisor classes. For the second equality, use <a href="#fr:Frob-pullback-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:Frob-pullback-graded">[fr:Frob-pullback-graded]</a>. Complementary degrees in <a href="#fr:socle-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:socle-pairing">[fr:socle-pairing]</a> have total first Chern class $e_j(p-1)K_W$. Summing gives $$\sum_jc_1(A_j(\Omega_W^1))=\frac{s(p-1)}2K_W.$$ Thus $c_1(F^*\mathcal E)=sB_p+s(p-1)K_W/2$, and Frobenius pullback of twisted divisor classes multiplies by $p$, as required.

For $0\le\lambda\le q$, the fixed movable inequality gives $$(D+K_W/2+\lambda S)\cdot\gamma\le40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma,$$ where pullback by $\pi_x$ is understood. This concerns only intersections of fixed divisors and the fixed complete-intersection witness, so it remains true on the reduction. The class in <a href="#fr:slot-determinant-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:slot-determinant-class">[fr:slot-determinant-class]</a> differs from its left-hand divisor at $\lambda=\lambda_i$ by $$\frac1p\bigl(B_p-pD-K_W/2\bigr).$$ Its numerator belongs to the fixed finite list $T_0-aD-K_W/2$, $a\in\{0,\ldots,k_0-1\}$. The resulting error after intersection with $\gamma$ is therefore $O(p^{-1})$, uniformly over the selected determinant columns.

<a id="fr:slot-order"></a>
Let $x'$ be the twisted point and let $0\ne\tau_i\in
H^0(W',\mathcal Q_i)$. The strict transform of its effective divisor on the blowup at $x'$ pairs nonnegatively with the twist of $\gamma$. Since $J\cdot\gamma>0$, it follows that $$\label{fr:slot-order}
 \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{x'}(\tau_i)}R\le40\epsilon+O(1/p).$$ The constant is uniform in the columns and in $\tau_i$. This argument tests sections on the reduction itself; it does not require them to lift to characteristic zero.

<a id="fr:determinant-order-upper"></a>
Künneth identifies the space containing $\sigma$ with $H^0(W',\mathcal Q_1)\otimes H^0(W',\mathcal Q_2)$. Choose bases adapted to the orders of vanishing at $x'$ in each factor. In each degree their leading terms are linearly independent. Tensor products of these leading terms remain independent in each bidegree of the two disjoint sets of parameters; terms of different bidegrees cannot cancel. Every nonzero tensor consequently has order at most the sum of the two maximum basis orders. Applying <a href="#fr:slot-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:slot-order">[fr:slot-order]</a>, we obtain $$\label{fr:determinant-order-upper}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\le R\bigl(80\epsilon+O(1/p)\bigr).$$

<a id="fr:determinant-order-lower"></a>
On the other hand, <a href="#fr:diagonal-rank-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:diagonal-rank-upper">[fr:diagonal-rank-upper]</a> and Lemma <a href="#fr:diagonal-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fr:diagonal-sections">24</a> bound the matrix rank at $(x',x')$ by a number strictly smaller than $R/4$. Trivialize its line bundles near that point. Invertible constant row operations make more than $3R/4$ rows vanish at the point. Each entry of those rows belongs to the maximal ideal, so every term of the determinant has order at least $3R/4$. Therefore $$\label{fr:determinant-order-lower}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\ge3R/4.$$ Equations <a href="#fr:determinant-order-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:determinant-order-upper">[fr:determinant-order-upper]</a> and <a href="#fr:determinant-order-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:determinant-order-lower">[fr:determinant-order-lower]</a> contradict $80\epsilon<3/4$ for sufficiently large $p$.

This contradiction proves Theorem <a href="#thm:finite-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite-data">20</a>. The full-rank calculation used $z_*$, while the diagonal estimate holds at every point and was tested at the separately chosen $x$. The errors were controlled by one fixed finite list of divisor classes and one fixed flag polynomial. Thus their constants do not depend on $p$, the filtration index, or the chosen columns.

<a id="fr:application"></a>
#### Application to the terminal case

We return to Theorem <a href="#thm:terminal-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:terminal-big">17</a>. Thus $X$ is a projective terminal $\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of dimension $n\ge2$ with $K_X\sim0$, and $L$ is a nef Cartier divisor of full nef dimension. Assume that $L$ is not big. Recall the ample rational divisors $P=r(L+tA)$, where $A$ is a fixed ample Cartier divisor and $t>0$ tends to zero, with $$r\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad P^n\longrightarrow\epsilon^n,
 \qquad \rho=(P^n)^{1/n}.$$ The positive rational number $\epsilon$ was chosen so that $(14\epsilon)^n<1/4$ and $80\epsilon<3/4$. Choose the integer $q$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:large-seshadri" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:large-seshadri">19</a>, and then choose $t$ sufficiently small that this proposition and Lemma <a href="#lemma:scalar-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:scalar-bound">18</a> apply, that $r>q+1$, and that $P^n<(2\epsilon)^n$. We now fix $t$, and therefore also $P,r,\rho$ and the projective bundle in <a href="#eq:bundle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bundle">[eq:bundle]</a>. Every remaining choice is made over $\mathbb C$ before reducing to positive characteristic.

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
###### The divisors and the flag.

Choose a smooth projective resolution $w:W\to X$ and set $$D=w^*P,\qquad S=w^*L,\qquad H_0=w^*P.$$ The divisor $S$ is integral Cartier, and $H_0$ is nef and big. Since $X$ is terminal and $K_X\sim0$, there is an integral effective $w$-exceptional divisor $E$ with $K_W\sim E$. The projection formula and the nefness of $P-rL$ give $$K_WH_0^{n-1}=0,\qquad
 (2D+qS)H_0^{n-1}\le(2+q/r)H_0^n.$$ At $H_0$, the inequalities $K_WH_0^{n-1}<2H_0^n/r$ and $(2D+qS)H_0^{n-1}<4H_0^n$ are strict. Thus, by continuity of intersection products, a sufficiently small rational ample perturbation $H=H_0+\eta A_W$, with $A_W$ an ample Cartier divisor on $W$ and $\eta>0$, satisfies $$H^n\le(2\epsilon)^n,\qquad
 K_WH^{n-1}\le2H^n/r,\qquad
 (2D+qS)H^{n-1}\le4H^n.$$ This gives <a href="#fr:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:intersection-bounds">[fr:intersection-bounds]</a>.

The canonical divisor of $W$ is pseudo-effective. Generic semipositivity of the cotangent bundle, originating in <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[Miyaoka](#ref-Miyaoka)] and used here in the form <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[CP](#ref-CP), Theorem 2.1], together with restriction to sufficiently general complete-intersection curves <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[MR](#ref-MR), Theorem 6.1], gives a smooth integral flag with successive divisor classes $l_iH$, for sufficiently large divisible integers $l_i$, such that $$\mu_{\min}(\Omega_W^1|_C)\ge0.$$ This is the flag in Hypothesis <span class="upright">(i)</span> of Theorem <a href="#thm:finite-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite-data">20</a>.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
###### The finite jet system.

On the bundle of <a href="#eq:bundle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bundle">[eq:bundle]</a>, write $R=P_1+P_2+q\xi$. The divisor $R$ is ample: $\xi$ is nef and relatively ample, and $P_1+P_2$ is the pullback of an ample divisor on $X\times X$. Proposition <a href="#prop:large-seshadri" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:large-seshadri">19</a> supplies a smooth point $z_*$ in the complement of the two axes with $$\varepsilon(R;z_*)>2n+2.$$ The point can be chosen over the open set where $w$ is an isomorphism. Choose a rational number $a$ strictly between $2n+2$ and $\varepsilon(R;z_*)$. On the blowup $b:\widetilde Z\to Z$ at $z_*$, with exceptional divisor $G$, the rational divisor $b^*R-aG$ is ample. For sufficiently large divisible $k_0$, Serre vanishing gives $$H^1\bigl(\widetilde Z,
          \mathcal O_{\widetilde Z}(k_0b^*R-k_0aG)\bigr)=0.$$ Since $z_*$ is smooth, the sections on the thickened exceptional divisor $k_0aG$ identify with the jet quotient at $z_*$ modulo $\mathfrak m_{z_*}^{k_0a}$, in a local frame for $k_0R$. The restriction sequence therefore shows that sections of $k_0R$ generate jets through order $k_0a-1$, and hence through order $(2n+2)k_0$. Increase the divisibility of $k_0$ to make $k_0P$ Cartier. Pulling these sections to $$Z_W=\mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal O_W(S)_1\oplus\mathcal O_W(S)_2\bigr)
       \longrightarrow W\times W$$ preserves the jets at the corresponding point, because the induced map $Z_W\to Z$ is an isomorphism near that point. The pulled-back divisor is $D_1+D_2+q\xi$. We have obtained the finite system required in Hypothesis <span class="upright">(ii)</span>.

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
###### The movable curve class.

<a id="fr:application-order"></a>
Choose a very general point $x\in W$ outside the exceptional locus, with image in the locus where Lemma <a href="#lemma:scalar-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:scalar-bound">18</a> holds. Let $\pi_x:\widehat W\to W$ be its blowup and $J$ its exceptional divisor. The rational divisor $$D^+=2D+E$$ is big. For every sufficiently divisible positive integer $m$, normality of $X$ and exceptionality of $E$ give $w_*\mathcal O_W(mE)=\mathcal O_X$. Thus every section of $mD^+$ comes from a section of $2mP$, multiplied by the section of the fixed divisor $mE$. Since $x\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}E$, Lemma <a href="#lemma:scalar-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:scalar-bound">18</a> implies $$\label{fr:application-order}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)/m\le8\rho<16\epsilon
 \qquad\bigl(0\ne s\in H^0(W,\mathcal O_W(mD^+))\bigr).$$

<a id="fr:application-not-pe"></a>
It follows that $$\label{fr:application-not-pe}
 \pi_x^*D^+-40\epsilon J\quad\text{is not pseudo-effective}.$$ Indeed, if it were pseudo-effective, a convex combination with the big divisor $\pi_x^*D^+$ would make $\pi_x^*D^+-cJ$ big for any rational $c$ with $16\epsilon<c<40\epsilon$. A nonzero section of a sufficiently divisible multiple would then give a section violating <a href="#fr:application-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:application-order">[fr:application-order]</a>.

By the duality between pseudo-effective divisors and strongly movable curves <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[BDPP](#ref-BDPP)], the strict separation in <a href="#fr:application-not-pe" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:application-not-pe">[fr:application-not-pe]</a> is detected by a class $$\gamma=f_*(A_1\cdots A_{n-1}),
 \qquad f:V\longrightarrow\widehat W,$$ where $V$ is smooth projective, $f$ is birational, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors. To obtain this form, first choose a generating strongly movable class with negative pairing, then approximate its ample real classes by rational ones and clear denominators. We have $$(\pi_x^*D^+)\cdot\gamma<40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma.$$ The left side is nonnegative, because $\pi_x^*D^+$ is pseudo-effective, so $J\cdot\gamma>0$.

For every $0\le\lambda\le q$, $$D^+-(D+K_W/2+\lambda S)
 \sim_{\mathbb R}w^*(P-\lambda L)+E/2$$ is pseudo-effective: $P-\lambda L=(P-rL)+(r-\lambda)L$ is nef and $E$ is effective. Pulling back to $\widehat W$ and pairing with $\gamma$ therefore gives $$\pi_x^*(D+K_W/2+\lambda S)\cdot\gamma
 \le(\pi_x^*D^+)\cdot\gamma
 <40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma.$$ This proves Hypothesis <span class="upright">(iii)</span>. All the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:finite-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite-data">20</a> now hold for one fixed collection of complex data, a contradiction. Hence $L$ is big, completing the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:terminal-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:terminal-big">17</a>.

<a id="sec:completion"></a>
### Completion of the full-dimension induction

Theorem <a href="#thm:terminal-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:terminal-big">17</a> treats varieties with terminal singularities and linearly trivial canonical divisor. We now pass to klt Calabi–Yau pairs and complete the full-dimension assertion. The boundary perturbation below follows the reduction in <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Section 7]: an effective representative of a small boundary perturbation plus the nef divisor allows ordinary log abundance to be applied to a multiple of that nef divisor.

<a id="stmt-prop-full-all"></a>
<div id="prop:full-all" class="proposition">

**Proposition 25**. *Let $n\geq 2$, and assume $(P_j)$ for every $j<n$, together with Theorems <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> and <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a>. Let $(X,B)$ be a projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair over $\mathbb C$ of dimension $n$, and let $M$ be a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. Suppose that $T=K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective and that, for some rational $a>0$, $$(T+aM)\cdot C>0$$ for every integral curve $C$ through a very general point of $X$. Then $T+cM$ is big for every rational $c>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-full-all"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-full-all</summary>

<a id="eq:completion-cy-claim"></a>
*Proof.* Proposition <a href="#prop:full-positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:full-positive">8</a> proves the assertion when $\kappa(T)>0$. Proposition <a href="#prop:reduction-cy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:reduction-cy">9</a> reduces the remaining case to the following claim in dimension $n$: $$\label{eq:completion-cy-claim}
 \begin{gathered}
 (X,B)\text{ projective klt},\qquad K_X+B\equiv 0,\\
 M\text{ nef and $\mathbb Q$-Cartier of full nef dimension}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad M\text{ big}.
 \end{gathered}$$ We prove this claim first with $B=0$ and then with arbitrary boundary. We may take a small projective $\mathbb Q$-factorialization throughout: the adjoint and $M$ pull back, full nef dimension is preserved on the isomorphism locus, and bigness of the pullback implies bigness downstairs.

*The case of zero boundary.* Suppose that $X$ is klt and $K_X\equiv0$. Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> makes $K_X$ semiample, hence $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. Indeed, a semiample numerically trivial divisor defines a morphism with zero-dimensional image, so a sufficiently divisible multiple is linearly trivial.

Take the global index-one cover $$\pi\colon X^{\mathrm{ind}}\longrightarrow X$$ associated with a trivialization of the smallest positive multiple of $K_X$ that is linearly trivial. This is a finite quasi-étale cover, and $$K_{X^{\mathrm{ind}}}=\pi^*K_X\sim0.$$ The cover is klt. Since its canonical divisor is Cartier, its log discrepancies are positive integers, hence at least one; it therefore has canonical singularities. These are the standard index-one cover properties for klt varieties; see <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[KM](#ref-KM), Definition 5.19 and Proposition 5.20]. By the crepant terminalization theorem <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[BCHM](#ref-BCHM), Corollary 1.4.3], there is a projective birational morphism $$h\colon \widetilde X\longrightarrow X^{\mathrm{ind}}$$ with $\widetilde X$ terminal and $\mathbb Q$-factorial and $K_{\widetilde X}=h^*K_{X^{\mathrm{ind}}}$. In particular, $K_{\widetilde X}$ is Cartier and linearly trivial, as required by Theorem <a href="#thm:terminal-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:terminal-big">17</a>.

Set $\widetilde M=(\pi\circ h)^*M$. This divisor is nef and has full nef dimension. To verify the latter assertion, choose a very general point upstairs outside the exceptional locus of $h$ and above the very general locus used for $M$. Every curve through this point has a curve as its image in $X$, and the projection formula gives strictly positive $\widetilde M$-degree. A positive Cartier multiple of $\widetilde M$ is therefore big by Theorem <a href="#thm:terminal-big" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:terminal-big">17</a>. Bigness is detected by a dominant generically finite pullback, so $M$ is big on $X$.

*Introducing the boundary.* Now let $(X,B)$ satisfy the hypotheses of <a href="#eq:completion-cy-claim" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completion-cy-claim">[eq:completion-cy-claim]</a>, with $B\ne0$. Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> gives $$K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0.$$ Choose a rational $\alpha>0$ sufficiently small that $(X,(1+\alpha)B)$ remains klt, and put $$T_*=K_X+(1+\alpha)B\sim_{\mathbb Q}\alpha B.$$ Thus $T_*$ is pseudo-effective and $\kappa(T_*)\geq0$. The full condition holds for $T_*$ and $M$: for a curve $C$ through a point outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}B$, we have $T_*\cdot C=\alpha B\cdot C\geq0$, whereas $M\cdot C>0$ if that point is very general. We will show that $T_*+M$ is big without using the conclusion of the present proposition.

If $\kappa(T_*)>0$, this follows from Proposition <a href="#prop:full-positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:full-positive">8</a>. Suppose instead that $\kappa(T_*)=0$, and apply Lemma <a href="#lemma:two-nef-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:two-nef-model">7</a> to $(X,(1+\alpha)B)$ and $M$. Write $Y$ for its final model, $B_Y$ and $N$ for the transforms of $B$ and $M$, and $$U=K_Y+(1+\alpha)B_Y.$$ The output has $U\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ and, for some rational $s>0$, the divisor $U+sN$ is nef of full nef dimension. The maps in the construction extract no divisors, so pushing forward the rational linear equivalence $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ also gives $$K_Y+B_Y\sim_{\mathbb Q}0.$$ Subtracting these two equivalences shows that $\alpha B_Y\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. Since $B_Y$ is effective on a projective variety, intersection with a sufficiently ample complete-intersection curve forces $B_Y=0$. The ordinary pair on $Y$ is therefore klt with zero boundary and $K_Y\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. Moreover, $N$ is nef of full nef dimension, because $U+sN\equiv sN$. The zero-boundary case just proved makes $N$, and hence $U+sN$, big.

On a common smooth resolution, with maps $p$ to $X$ and $q$ to $Y$, the effective exceptional comparison from the reduction is $$p^*(T_*+sM)=q^*(U+sN)+E,
  \qquad E\geq0.$$ It follows that $T_*+sM$ is big. Choose a positive rational $\lambda$ with $\lambda<\min\{1,1/s\}$. Then $$T_*+M
   =\lambda(T_*+sM)+(1-\lambda)T_*+(1-\lambda s)M$$ is big, since the first summand is big and the other two are pseudo-effective. We have thus established bigness of $T_*+M$ in both cases.

Choose an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $G$ with $$G\sim_{\mathbb Q}T_*+M\sim_{\mathbb Q}\alpha B+M.$$ For a sufficiently small rational $u>0$, the divisor $$\Delta_u=(1-u\alpha)B+uG$$ is effective and $(X,\Delta_u)$ is klt. To see this explicitly, take $u\alpha<1$ and choose $u$ small enough that $(X,B+uG)$ is klt on a fixed log resolution; then $\Delta_u\leq B+uG$. Its adjoint satisfies $$K_X+\Delta_u
   \sim_{\mathbb Q} K_X+B+u(G-\alpha B)
   \sim_{\mathbb Q}uM.$$ This adjoint is nef, so Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a> makes it semiample. Consequently $M$ itself is semiample. The morphism given by a free multiple of $M$ has zero-dimensional general fiber: a positive-dimensional fiber through a very general point would contain a curve of $M$-degree zero, contrary to full nef dimension. That morphism is therefore generically finite onto its image, and $M$ is big. This proves <a href="#eq:completion-cy-claim" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:completion-cy-claim">[eq:completion-cy-claim]</a> for all klt Calabi–Yau pairs.

Proposition <a href="#prop:reduction-cy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:reduction-cy">9</a> now supplies the case $\kappa(T)=0$, while Proposition <a href="#prop:full-positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:full-positive">8</a> supplies $\kappa(T)>0$. These exhaust the possibilities furnished by the minimal-model and abundance inputs for a pseudo-effective klt adjoint, and establish the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:positive"></a>
### Positive parts and numerical semiampleness

We complete the simultaneous induction by proving the following positive-part statement.

<a id="stmt-prop-positive-part"></a>
<div id="prop:positive-part" class="proposition">

**Proposition 26**. *For every projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pair $(X,B)$ over $\mathbb C$ with $K_X+B$ pseudo-effective and every nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $M$ on $X$, there is a smooth projective birational model $w\colon W\to X$ such that $$P_\sigma\bigl(w^*(K_X+B+M)\bigr)$$ is rational and numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-divisor.*

</div>

For the inductive step, fix such $(X,B)$ and $M$ with $\dim X=n\geq2$, and put $T=K_X+B$. We assume $(Z_j)$ for $j<n$ and use $(P_n)$ from Proposition <a href="#prop:full-all" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:full-all">25</a>. Following the positive-part method of Lazić–Peternell <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Theorem 5.3], we first make a sufficiently large adjoint nef. Full nef dimension then gives bigness by $(P_n)$; otherwise its nef reduction permits the nef summand to descend to a lower-dimensional base.

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
#### Positive parts under the required comparisons

We will repeatedly use the following properties of Nakayama’s divisorial Zariski decomposition <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[Nakayama](#ref-Nakayama)].

<a id="stmt-lemma-positive-properties"></a>
<div id="lemma:positive-properties" class="lemma">

**Lemma 27**. *The following assertions hold.*

1.  *Let $p\colon V\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a smooth projective variety to a normal projective variety. If $D$ is a pseudo-effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$ and $E$ is an effective $p$-exceptional rational divisor, then $$P_\sigma(p^*D+E)=P_\sigma(p^*D).$$*

2.  *Let $f\colon V\to S$ be a surjective morphism of smooth projective varieties, and let $D$ be a pseudo-effective rational divisor on $S$. If $P_\sigma(D)$ is nef, then $$P_\sigma(f^*D)=f^*P_\sigma(D).$$*

3.  *For rational divisors on a fixed smooth projective variety, rationality and numerical semiampleness of the positive part depend only on the numerical class of the divisor.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-positive-properties"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-positive-properties</summary>

*Proof.* The first two statements are <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Lemmas 2.4 and 2.5]; the first allows the target $X$ to be normal. For the third, each asymptotic divisorial order $\sigma_\Gamma(D)$ depends only on the numerical class. Thus numerically equivalent divisors have the same negative part as an actual real divisor. For rational $D,D'$ with $D\equiv D'$, $$P_\sigma(D')-P_\sigma(D)=D'-D.$$ Consequently one positive part is rational if and only if the other is, and the two positive parts are numerically equivalent. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:positive-transfer"></a>
In particular, a positive part that is rational and numerically semiample is nef, so these properties persist on higher smooth models. Suppose that on a common smooth resolution of a birational contraction one has $$\label{eq:positive-transfer}
                         p^*D\equiv q^*D'+E,$$ where $D,D'$ are pseudo-effective rational Cartier divisors and $E$ is effective and $q$-exceptional. If a smooth model over the target has rational numerically semiample positive part for $D'$, take a higher common resolution dominating that model. The second part of Lemma <a href="#lemma:positive-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:positive-properties">27</a> pulls that positive part to the common resolution. Its first and third parts, applied to <a href="#eq:positive-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-transfer">[eq:positive-transfer]</a>, give the same conclusion for $D$. This is the comparison that will be used at each birational step.

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### An MMP trivial on the nef divisor

<a id="stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint"></a>
<div id="lemma:large-nef-adjoint" class="lemma">

**Lemma 28**. *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt $\mathbb Q$-pair of dimension $n$, with $T=K_X+B$ pseudo-effective, and let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor on $X$. If $m$ is an integer greater than $2n$, there is a terminating $(T+mL)$-MMP whose every step is $L$-trivial and is an ordinary $T$-negative step. On its output, the transform of $L$ is Cartier and nef, and the transform of $T+mL$ is nef.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint</summary>

*Proof.* Use Theorem <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a> for the generalized klt pair whose nef data are $mL$ on $X$. Its adjoint $T+mL$ is pseudo-effective. Suppose inductively that on the current model the ordinary pair is klt and the transform, still denoted by $L$, is Cartier and nef. Every $(T+mL)$-negative extremal ray is $T$-negative. By the ordinary length bound, it has a rational curve generator $C$ with $0<-T\cdot C\leq2n$. If $L\cdot C>0$, integrality gives $$(T+mL)\cdot C\geq-2n+m>0,$$ which is impossible. Hence $L\cdot C=0$.

The Cartier descent statement in the ordinary cone and contraction theorem gives a Cartier divisor on the contraction base whose pullback is $L$. It is nef: lift any curve on the base to a curve dominating it and use the projection formula. In a flip its pullback to the flipped variety is therefore again Cartier and nef. The relative signs of $T+mL$ are those of $T$, so the flip is also an ordinary flip for the klt pair. Thus the inductive hypotheses persist. The terminating program supplied by Theorem <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a> has all the stated properties. ◻

</details>

Apply the lemma after a small $\mathbb Q$-factorialization, with $L=m_0M$ for an integer $m_0>0$ making $L$ Cartier. The program is $M$-trivial, so on a common resolution its ordinary adjoint comparison also gives $$p^*(T+M)=q^*(T'+M')+E,$$ where $E$ is effective and exceptional over the output. The transformed ordinary adjoint $T'$ remains pseudo-effective, and $M'$ is nef. By <a href="#eq:positive-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-transfer">[eq:positive-transfer]</a>, it suffices to prove $(Z_n)$ on that output. We may consequently retain the notation $X,B,T,M,L$ and assume in addition that <a id="eq:large-adjoint-nef"></a>$$\label{eq:large-adjoint-nef}
                               T+mL\text{ is nef}.$$

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
#### The case of full nef dimension

<a id="eq:big-ordinary-boundary"></a>
Suppose first that $T+mL$ has full nef dimension. Then <a href="#eq:full-condition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-condition">[eq:full-condition]</a> holds, and $(P_n)$ shows that $T+M$ is big. Write, by Kodaira’s lemma, $$T+M\sim_\mathbb QA_1+G_1,$$ where $A_1$ is ample and $G_1\geq0$ is rational. Choose a rational $v>0$ sufficiently small that $(X,B+vG_1)$ is klt. Since $M+vA_1$ is ample, a general effective rational representative $A'$ of this divisor can be chosen with sufficiently small coefficients that $(X,B+vG_1+A')$ is klt. Its adjoint satisfies $$\label{eq:big-ordinary-boundary}
 K_X+B+vG_1+A'\sim_\mathbb Q(1+v)(T+M).$$ It is big and has a good minimal model: one may use the big-case theorem of <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[BCHM](#ref-BCHM)], or combine Theorems <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a> and <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a>. On a common resolution, the left side of <a href="#eq:big-ordinary-boundary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:big-ordinary-boundary">[eq:big-ordinary-boundary]</a> is the pullback of a semiample divisor plus an effective exceptional divisor. Its positive part is therefore the semiample pullback. Dividing by $1+v$ proves $(Z_n)$ in this case.

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
#### Descent along a nef reduction

We treat the remaining case, where the nef dimension of $T+mL$ is strictly smaller than $n$. Let $$X\dashrightarrow Z$$ be its nef reduction, with $Z$ normal and projective. A very general compact fibre $F$ is contained in the morphism locus and has positive dimension. On this fibre, $$(T+mL)|_F\equiv0.$$ The restriction $T|_F$ is pseudo-effective. To see this, choose effective rational approximations to $T$ after adding positive ample perturbations tending to zero, and choose $F$ outside the countably many exceptional choices for these approximations. Their restrictions are effective, and their classes approach $T|_F$. After a resolution of $F$ the same argument applies to the pullbacks. As $L|_F$ is nef and $T|_F\equiv-mL|_F$, both divisors are numerically trivial. Indeed, intersect this equality with an ample complete-intersection curve on a smooth resolution of $F$. Pseudo-effectivity makes the left side nonnegative and nefness makes the right side nonpositive. Their common value is zero, and a nef divisor with zero intersection against an ample complete-intersection curve is numerically trivial.

<a id="eq:fibre-adjoint-trivial"></a>
Restriction to a sufficiently general fibre gives a klt pair $(F,B|_F)$ with adjoint $T|_F$. By Theorem <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a>, its numerically trivial adjoint is semiample, hence $$\label{eq:fibre-adjoint-trivial}
                         K_F+B|_F\sim_\mathbb Q0.$$ These restrictions are made where the almost holomorphic nef reduction is a morphism. Normality and the klt restriction statement hold after shrinking the base; alternatively they follow by restricting a log resolution over a general smooth open. Numerical triviality, initially obtained for very general fibres, also follows on a general fibre by the constancy of the relevant intersection numbers in this family.

<a id="eq:nef-descent"></a>
We next use the birational descent of nef divisors <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Lemma 3.1]. After resolving the nef reduction and modifying its base birationally, it supplies a diagram $$X\xleftarrow{p}X_1\xrightarrow{f}Z_1$$ in which $X_1,Z_1$ are smooth and projective, $p$ is birational, $f$ is a contraction, and there is a rational divisor $M_1$ on $Z_1$ with $$\label{eq:nef-descent}
                              p^*M\equiv f^*M_1.$$ The divisor $M_1$ is nef, since its pullback is numerically equivalent to the nef divisor $p^*M$ and nefness descends under a surjective morphism. We may take a further log resolution of the pair on top without affecting <a href="#eq:nef-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:nef-descent">[eq:nef-descent]</a>.

<a id="eq:resolved-boundary"></a>
Write the crepant boundary as $B_1^+-B_1^-$, with effective rational divisors of disjoint support, so that $$K_{X_1}+B_1^+-B_1^-=p^*(K_X+B).$$ Set $B_1=B_1^+$ and $E=B_1^-$. Since the original boundary is effective, $E$ is $p$-exceptional. Klt singularities make all coefficients of $B_1$ less than one, and its support is simple normal crossings. Thus $(X_1,B_1)$ is klt and $$\label{eq:resolved-boundary}
                    K_{X_1}+B_1=p^*T+E,\qquad E\geq0.$$ In particular its adjoint is pseudo-effective.

<a id="eq:fibre-kappa-zero"></a>
If $F_1$ is a general fibre of $f$, it maps birationally onto the corresponding compact fibre $F$ of the nef reduction. Every component of $E$ that meets $F_1$ restricts to an exceptional divisor over $F$: for a centre dominating the base, codimension at least two is preserved on the general fibre, and the other centres are avoided. Equations <a href="#eq:fibre-adjoint-trivial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fibre-adjoint-trivial">[eq:fibre-adjoint-trivial]</a> and <a href="#eq:resolved-boundary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolved-boundary">[eq:resolved-boundary]</a> therefore show that $$\label{eq:fibre-kappa-zero}
               \kappa\bigl(F_1,(K_{X_1}+B_1)|_{F_1}\bigr)=0.$$ The restriction is an effective exceptional divisor up to rational linear equivalence. Equivalently, its section spaces are those of the trivial divisor on the normal fibre $F$. In particular the general fibre pair has pseudo-effective adjoint and has a good minimal model by Theorems <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a> and <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-43"></a>
#### A relative good model and a lower-dimensional adjoint

At this point the nef summand comes from $Z_1$, while the ordinary adjoint has Iitaka dimension zero on its general fibres. We use a relative good model to express that ordinary adjoint as a pullback from a variety of dimension less than $n$.

The relative log canonical ring of the rational klt pair $(X_1,B_1)$ over $Z_1$ is finitely generated. More precisely, for a positive integer $r$ making $r(K_{X_1}+B_1)$ Cartier, its Veronese algebra $$\bigoplus_{\ell\geq0}
 f_*\mathcal O_{X_1}\bigl(\ell r(K_{X_1}+B_1)\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $\mathcal O_{Z_1}$-algebra. This is the relative finite-generation theorem for rational klt pairs, in the form <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[FujinoFG](#ref-FujinoFG), Theorem 1.4], obtained from the finite-generation theorem of <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[BCHM](#ref-BCHM)] and the canonical-bundle reduction of Fujino–Mori <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[FM](#ref-FM), Theorem 5.2]. Together with the good minimal models of the very general closed fibres established above, this verifies both hypotheses of <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[HX](#ref-HX), Theorem 2.12]. That theorem gives a good minimal model over $Z_1$; the termination statement for an MMP with scaling once a good model exists, <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[HX](#ref-HX), Corollary 2.9], supplies a relative program $$(X_1,B_1)\dashrightarrow(X_2,B_2).$$ Thus $X_2$ is projective and $\mathbb Q$-factorial, $(X_2,B_2)$ is klt, and $K_{X_2}+B_2$ is semiample over $Z_1$. This application uses very general closed fibres over $\mathbb C$ and requires no change of the ground field to a geometric generic field.

By <a href="#eq:nef-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:nef-descent">[eq:nef-descent]</a>, we may replace the nef term numerically by $f^*M_1$ before making the positive-part comparisons. This pullback is trivial on every step of the relative program and is carried unchanged from the base. The ordinary adjoint remains pseudo-effective by numerical pushforward.

<a id="eq:relative-iitaka"></a>
Let $$X_2\xrightarrow{g}Z_2\xrightarrow{j}Z_1$$ be the relative semiample fibration of $K_{X_2}+B_2$, with $g$ a contraction. Its construction gives a $j$-ample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $A_2$ on $Z_2$ such that $$\label{eq:relative-iitaka}
                         K_{X_2}+B_2\sim_\mathbb Qg^*A_2.$$ The relative program preserves Iitaka dimension on the general fibres, by its effective exceptional pullback comparisons. From <a href="#eq:fibre-kappa-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fibre-kappa-zero">[eq:fibre-kappa-zero]</a>, the relative Iitaka dimension is zero. Hence $$\dim Z_2=\dim Z_1<n.$$

<a id="eq:base-adjoint"></a>
Apply Ambro’s canonical bundle formula consequence <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[Ambro](#ref-Ambro), Theorem 0.2] to <a href="#eq:relative-iitaka" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:relative-iitaka">[eq:relative-iitaka]</a>. Its hypotheses hold: $(X_2,B_2)$ is a projective klt pair with effective rational boundary, $g$ is a contraction to a normal projective variety, and the ordinary adjoint is rationally linearly pulled back from a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. It gives an effective rational boundary $B_{Z_2}$ such that $(Z_2,B_{Z_2})$ is klt and $$\label{eq:base-adjoint}
              K_{X_2}+B_2\sim_\mathbb Qg^*(K_{Z_2}+B_{Z_2}).$$ The base adjoint is pseudo-effective. One direct verification, using only the inputs already in force, is as follows. The pseudo-effective ordinary adjoint on $X_2$ has a good minimal model by Theorems <a href="#input:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:models">5</a> and <a href="#input:abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="input:abundance">4</a>; it therefore has a nonzero section in a sufficiently divisible positive multiple. Since $g_*\mathcal O_{X_2}=\mathcal O_{Z_2}$, the projection formula and <a href="#eq:base-adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-adjoint">[eq:base-adjoint]</a> identify this section space with the corresponding section space on $Z_2$. Thus $K_{Z_2}+B_{Z_2}$ even has an effective rationally linearly equivalent divisor.

We are now in the setting of $(Z_{\dim Z_2})$: the pair on the base is klt with pseudo-effective adjoint, and $j^*M_1$ is a nef rational Cartier divisor. There is consequently a smooth projective birational model $w_2\colon W_2\to Z_2$ on which $$P_\sigma\bigl(w_2^*(K_{Z_2}+B_{Z_2}+j^*M_1)\bigr)$$ is rational and numerically semiample. Resolve the main component of $X_2\times_{Z_2}W_2$ to obtain a smooth projective variety $V$ mapping birationally to $X_2$ and surjectively to $W_2$. Since the displayed positive part is nef, the pullback formula of Lemma <a href="#lemma:positive-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:positive-properties">27</a>, together with <a href="#eq:base-adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-adjoint">[eq:base-adjoint]</a>, proves the same positive-part assertion on $V$ for $$K_{X_2}+B_2+g^*j^*M_1.$$

Take a common resolution dominating both $V$ and $X_1$. The relative MMP compares $K_{X_1}+B_1$ with $K_{X_2}+B_2$ by adding an effective divisor exceptional over $X_2$. The $M_1$ terms have equal pullbacks, since they come from the relative base. The comparison <a href="#eq:positive-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-transfer">[eq:positive-transfer]</a> therefore proves the positive-part assertion on a smooth model over $X_1$ for $K_{X_1}+B_1+f^*M_1$. Finally, <a href="#eq:nef-descent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:nef-descent">[eq:nef-descent]</a> and <a href="#eq:resolved-boundary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolved-boundary">[eq:resolved-boundary]</a> give $$K_{X_1}+B_1+f^*M_1\equiv p^*(T+M)+E,$$ where $E$ is effective and $p$-exceptional. Another application of Lemma <a href="#lemma:positive-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:positive-properties">27</a> proves $(Z_n)$ on a smooth model over the original $X$.

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-large-nef-adjoint</summary>

*Completion of the proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:positive-part" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:positive-part">26</a>.* The preceding argument proves $(Z_n)$ from $(P_n)$ and the lower-dimensional assertions. Together with Proposition <a href="#prop:full-all" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:full-all">25</a> and the dimension-zero and dimension-one cases, it completes the simultaneous induction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-44"></a>
#### Descent to the original variety

The positive-part statement is birational, whereas the main theorem requires a semiample representative on the original variety. The following descent uses the rational singularities of klt pairs; it is the numerical semiampleness statement of <a id="pm-cite-69"></a>[[LP](#ref-LP), Lemma 2.14].

<a id="stmt-lemma-numsemiample-descent"></a>
<div id="lemma:numsemiample-descent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 29**. *Let $p\colon W\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a smooth projective variety to a normal projective variety with rational singularities, over $\mathbb C$. Let $D$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If $p^*D$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-divisor on $W$, then $D$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-numsemiample-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-numsemiample-descent</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a semiample rational divisor $A_W$ with $A_W\equiv p^*D$. After clearing denominators, the difference of the corresponding line bundles is numerically trivial. Numerically trivial line bundles modulo $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(W)$ are torsion. Thus, after increasing a positive integer $r$, we have $$\mathcal O_W(rA_W)\simeq p^*\mathcal O_X(rD)\otimes P_W
                 \quad\text{with }P_W\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(W).$$ Rational singularities identify $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(W)$ by pullback. Write $P_W=p^*P_X$ and set $\mathcal A_X=\mathcal O_X(rD)\otimes P_X$. Then $p^*\mathcal A_X\simeq\mathcal O_W(rA_W)$.

A positive power of the line bundle on the right is globally generated. Since $p_*\mathcal O_W=\mathcal O_X$, the projection formula identifies its sections with those of the same power of $\mathcal A_X$. These sections generate downstairs: a failure at any point of $X$ would remain a failure at every point above it. Hence $\mathcal A_X$ is semiample. A Cartier divisor representing $\mathcal A_X$, divided by $r$, is semiample and numerically equivalent to $D$, because $P_X$ is algebraically trivial. ◻

</details>

We can now prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb C$. Its divisor $D=K_X+B+M$ is nef, so $$P_\sigma(w^*D)=w^*D$$ on every smooth birational model. Proposition <a href="#prop:positive-part" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:positive-part">26</a> makes this pullback numerically semiample. A klt variety has rational singularities, so Lemma <a href="#lemma:numsemiample-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:numsemiample-descent">29</a> gives the required semiample rational Cartier divisor on $X$.

<a id="pm-h-45"></a>
#### Algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero

We finish by transferring the result to the ground fields in the statement of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The very general points used in the proof were needed only over $\mathbb C$; numerical semiampleness itself descends by a finite-type parameter argument.

<a id="stmt-lemma-field-transfer"></a>
<div id="lemma:field-transfer" class="lemma">

**Lemma 30**. *Let $k_0\subset K$ be an extension of algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. Let $X_0$ be a normal projective variety over $k_0$, and let $D_0$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor. If its pullback $D_K$ to $X_K$ is numerically equivalent to a semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor, then $D_0$ has that property over $k_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-field-transfer"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-field-transfer</summary>

*Proof.* Clear denominators in $D_0$ and in a semiample representative over $K$, and take a further multiple which is globally generated. Its difference from the corresponding multiple of $D_K$ is numerically trivial. After another positive multiple this difference belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X_K)$, since numerical triviality modulo $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0$ is torsion. We have therefore fixed an integer $r>0$ and a line bundle $P_K\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X_K)$ such that $$\mathcal O_{X_K}(rD_K)\otimes P_K$$ is globally generated.

Choose a $k_0$-point of $X_0$ and rigidify line bundles there. The finite-type scheme $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(X_0)$ then has a Poincaré family. Tensor this family by the fixed line bundle $\mathcal O_{X_0}(rD_0)$. The set of parameters for which the resulting line bundle is globally generated is constructible. Indeed, stratify the parameter scheme so that the zeroth direct image is locally free and cohomology and base change hold. On each stratum use the evaluation map; the image in the parameter space of the support of its cokernel is closed, because projection from $X_0$ is proper. Its complement is the globally generated locus on that stratum.

Formation of the Picard scheme and of this constructible condition commutes with the field extension. The locus is nonempty after extension to $K$, by the chosen $P_K$, and is consequently nonempty over $k_0$. As $k_0$ is algebraically closed, it has a $k_0$-point. The corresponding globally generated line bundle, represented by a Cartier divisor and divided by $r$, is the desired semiample rational divisor numerically equivalent to $D_0$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-field-transfer-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-field-transfer</summary>

*Completion of the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Let $k$ be the given algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Descend $X,B,M$, the required rational Cartier structures, and a projective embedding to a finitely generated subfield of $k$. Let $k_0\subset k$ be its algebraic closure inside $k$, and choose an embedding $k_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$.

The hypotheses persist under the resulting algebraically closed field extensions. For klt singularities this follows from a log resolution, which can be included among the descended data. Nefness is invariant under field extension, since it can be tested by ampleness after arbitrarily small rational ample perturbations. Pseudo-effectivity is invariant for the same reason with bigness in place of ampleness: a rational divisor is pseudo-effective exactly when all its positive rational ample perturbations are big, and bigness is preserved and reflected by extension of the section spaces. These tests show both that the descended data satisfy the hypotheses and that their complex extension does.

The result over $\mathbb C$ supplies a semiample numerical representative of $K_X+B+M$ after this extension. Lemma <a href="#lemma:field-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:field-transfer">30</a> descends it to $k_0$. Extending that representative to $k$ preserves global generation of a suitable multiple and numerical equivalence. It is the required semiample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on the original variety. ◻

</details>

      "explanation": "Both works establish numerical semiampleness for nef adjoint divisors formed by adding a nef line bundle to a pseudo effective log canonical divisor in algebraic and geometric settings.",
      "relation": "shared_technique",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-036.2",
      "source_quote": "Suppose that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, and let $M$ be a nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$. If $$D=K_X+B+M$$ is nef, then there is a semiample $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $X$ such that $D\\equiv L$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-036.1",
      "target_quote": "Assume that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, $M$ is nef, and $D=K_X+B+M$ is nef. Then there is a semiample rational line bundle $L$ on $X$ such that $$c_1(L)=c_1(D)\\quad\\text{in }H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(X,\\mathbb R).$$",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The numerical semiampleness theorem for generalized pairs relies on the minimal model program for generalized log canonical pairs with nef b-divisors to construct birational models whose log canonical part is nef.",

      "source_pm_id": "PM-036.2",

      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
